The metric tensor g_μν is a dynamical variable in the theory, but the underlying manifold structure — the spacetime substrate on which g_μν is defined — is fixed and non-dynamical. Yang-Mills 1954 is built on the static geometry of a principal bundle: a fixed base manifold with a fixed gauge group G and a connection A that is dynamical, but the bundle structure itself is a static mathematical stage. Kaluza-Klein 1921 is built on the static geometry of a five-dimensional manifold compactified on a circle; the five-dimensional structure is fixed, and the four-dimensional physics emerges as a projection. Differential-geometric formulations of classical mechanics from Cartan onward are built on the static geometry of symplectic manifolds and Poisson structures. Hestenes’s geometric algebra identifies the imaginary unit i with a unit bivector on static Minkowski spacetime — genuine geometry for i, but on a fixed geometric stage. Twistor theory is built on the static geometry of CP³. Loop quantum gravity is built on the static geometry of spin networks.

In every case, the geometry is the stage. The Lagrangian is the play performed on that stage. The stage does not move.

ℒ_McG is built differently. The stage itself is moving. dx₄/dt = ic is the assertion that the fourth axis of spacetime is not a fixed coordinate but an advancing physical quantity, expanding spherically symmetrically from every event at the velocity of light. The Lagrangian ℒ_McG is what the advance of the fourth axis looks like when projected onto the three spatial dimensions and interpreted through matter, gauge, and gravitational couplings. The four-fold uniqueness theorem of §VI is what forces the projection to take the specific form it does. The geometry is not a stage on which physics is performed; the geometry is the performance, and physics is its observable content in three dimensions.

The consequences of this architectural shift are throughout the paper. Every appearance of the imaginary unit i in the equations of physics — in the Minkowski metric through x₄ = ict, in the canonical commutation relation [q̂, p̂] = iℏ, in the Schrödinger equation iℏ∂ψ/∂t = Ĥψ, in the path-integral phase e^(iS/ℏ), in the +iε prescription, in the Dirac equation (iγ^μ∂_μ − m)ψ = 0, in the Heisenberg equation dA/dt = (i/ℏ)[H, A], in the Wick rotation t → −iτ, in the Fourier kernel, in the Fresnel integral, in the unitary evolution operator, in the Euclidean-Minkowski action relation — is the algebraic signature of x₄’s perpendicularity to the three spatial dimensions, inherited from the dynamical postulate dx₄/dt = ic. Every appearance of ℏ is the action per oscillatory step of x₄’s expansion at the Planck frequency. Every appearance of c is the rate of that expansion. The three constants of twentieth-century physics (c, ℏ, and the perpendicularity marker i) are three aspects of one dynamical principle. A static geometric Lagrangian cannot produce this unification because static geometry does not have a rate; dynamical geometry does, and the rate is c.

The historical pattern the McGucken Principle continues is the pattern of mathematical devices becoming physical realities. Planck’s 1900 introduction of E = hf was a bookkeeping trick; Einstein’s 1905 photoelectric paper elevated it to a physical statement about quanta of light. Minkowski’s 1908 introduction of x₄ = ict was a bookkeeping trick; the McGucken Principle elevates it to a physical statement about the fourth dimension’s advance. Schrödinger’s insertion of i into his 1926 wave equation was a pragmatic move to match atomic spectra; ℒ_McG reveals the i as the geometric signature of x₄’s perpendicularity. The pattern — formal device becoming physical reality through identification of the mechanism it was secretly describing — is the recurrent creative move of foundational physics. The McGucken Principle continues this pattern on the specific axis of Minkowski’s x₄, and ℒ_McG is the Lagrangian whose form makes that identification explicit.

No prior Lagrangian takes dynamical geometry as its postulate. The Einstein-Hilbert action is the closest precedent in the sense that it is the first Lagrangian forced by geometric considerations alone (diffeomorphism invariance plus second-order requirement), but Einstein-Hilbert’s geometry is the static Lorentzian manifold, not a dynamical equation of motion for a geometric quantity. ℒ_McG is the first Lagrangian whose postulate is itself an equation of motion for a geometric quantity — dx₄/dt = ic, the advance of the fourth axis at rate ic — and whose complete four-sector form is forced by that equation of motion. Dynamical geometric foundation, complete four-sector scope, single-postulate derivation. The three features together define a Lagrangian-architecture that has no prior instance in the 282-year lineage. ℒ_McG is the first.

## VIII.21 What ℒ_McG Does That the Standard Model Does Not Do

The Standard Model is extraordinarily successful at what it does. It predicts essentially all non-gravitational particle physics data to percent-level or better accuracy, its gauge structure was confirmed by the Higgs discovery in 2012, its CP-violation predictions match B-meson and kaon experiments to high precision, and its flavor-physics predictions hold across decades of precision measurements. None of this empirics is threatened by ℒ_McG. The form of ℒ_McG reproduces the Standard Model’s empirical predictions because the four sector-level uniqueness subtheorems of §VI force the same form that experiment has already confirmed. What ℒ_McG does that the Standard Model does not is a separate question, and it has a specific answer on the thirteen axes enumerated below.

### VIII.21.1 Forcing Versus Empirical Assembly

The Standard Model Lagrangian is a composite specified by selections at each level. The gauge group is chosen as SU(3)_c × SU(2)_L × U(1)_Y, selected among infinitely many compact Lie groups by matching observed matter content. The fermion representations are chosen to match observation — three generations, specific charge assignments, specific chirality patterns. The Higgs representation is chosen as an SU(2)_L doublet with hypercharge 1/2. The Yukawa couplings are fit to observed fermion masses. The CKM and PMNS matrix elements are measured rather than predicted. The composite has roughly nineteen free parameters plus the choices above, each an independent empirical input. ℒ_McG forces the form of all four sectors — free-particle kinetic, Dirac matter, Yang-Mills gauge, Einstein-Hilbert gravitational — from dx₄/dt = ic via Theorem VI.1. The form is derived, not selected. The specific gauge group G = U(1) × SU(2) × SU(3) is derived in [MG-QFT, §9]; given any compact Lie group G, Proposition VI.2 forces the kinetic Lagrangian’s form, and the geometric readings of the three factors in [MG-Noether] and [MG-Broken], completed there, reduce the remaining empirics to the specific group choice rather than the full form.

### VIII.21.2 Gravity Is Inside Rather Than Outside

The Standard Model does not contain gravity. It is a theory of non-gravitational physics on flat Minkowski spacetime, with Einstein-Hilbert bolted on as a separate classical theory that cannot be consistently combined with the Standard Model’s quantum structure at loop level. The composite Standard Model plus Einstein-Hilbert is two theories side by side. ℒ_McG contains gravity as one of the four sectors forced by the same postulate that forces matter, gauge, and free-particle sectors. The gravitational coupling G, the speed of light c, and Planck’s constant ℏ are related by the geometric structure of x₄’s advance at the Planck frequency, giving ℏ = ℓ_P² c³/G as a identity rather than a dimensional coincidence.

### VIII.21.3 The i, the ℏ, and the c Are Unified

In the Standard Model, the imaginary unit i appears in the Schrödinger equation, the canonical commutation relation, the Dirac equation, the Feynman path integral weight, the +iε prescription for propagators, the Wick rotation, the Heisenberg equation of motion, the Fourier transform kernel, the Fresnel integral, the unitary evolution operator, the complex wave function, and the Euclidean-Minkowski action relation — twelve distinct insertions cataloged in [MG-Wick, §V.5], each justified locally for its own technical reason (Hermiticity, unitarity, convergence, analytic continuation). No single account within the Standard Model explains why i is the universal factor across all of these equations. In ℒ_McG, every appearance of i traces to x₄ = ict as the perpendicularity marker of the fourth axis to the three spatial dimensions. The ℏ appearing throughout quantum mechanics is the action per oscillatory step of x₄’s expansion at the Planck frequency. The c appearing throughout relativity is the rate of x₄’s advance. Twelve distinct insertions of i by hand across twentieth-century physics reduce to one geometric source.

### VIII.21.4 Quantum Mechanics Is Forced, Not Inherited

The Standard Model takes the Hilbert space structure, the canonical commutation relation [q̂, p̂] = iℏ, the Born rule P = |ψ|², and unitary time evolution as inherited postulates of quantum mechanics — a separate theory sitting underneath the Standard Model, accepted without derivation. ℒ_McG’s companion corpus derives each as a theorem: the CCR from the operator-route and path-integral-route derivations of [MG-Commut] closed off against non-quantum alternatives by the Stone-von Neumann uniqueness theorem; the Born rule from the three-theorem structure of [MG-Born] identifying the complex character of ψ, the uniqueness of the squared modulus, and the geometric-overlap interpretation; the Schrödinger equation from the eight-step derivation of [MG-HLA] starting from the master equation u^μu_μ = −c² and concluding with iℏ∂ψ/∂t = Ĥψ; the path integral from the Huygens-cascade derivation of [MG-PathInt] identifying all paths as iterated McGucken Sphere expansions. The quantum structure of ℒ_McG is not inherited; it is forced by the same postulate that forces the Lagrangian.

### VIII.21.5 The Arrows of Time Are Unified

The Standard Model has no mechanism linking microscopic T-violation (the CKM phase as a free parameter) to macroscopic irreversibility (the Past Hypothesis as an independent cosmological boundary condition). They are two separate phenomena with two separate explanations. ℒ_McG locks them together through the sign of dx₄/dt. The directed expansion dx₄/dt = +ic (not −ic) breaks T, C, and P individually at the microscopic level. The same directed expansion drives the thermodynamic arrow, the radiative arrow, the quantum-measurement arrow, the cosmological arrow, and the matter-antimatter arrow as projections of one geometric fact. A counterfactual universe with dx₄/dt = −ic would be simultaneously antimatter-dominated, time-reversed, and oppositely CP-asymmetric — all three features flipping together because they share a source. The arrows of time — five in the count used throughout this paper (thermodynamic, radiative, cosmological, causal, psychological), seven in the extended catalog of [MG-Broken, §XI], which adds the quantum-measurement and matter-antimatter arrows — reduce to one directional fact in ℒ_McG.

### VIII.21.6 The Strong CP Problem Dissolves

In the Standard Model, θ_QCD is a free parameter whose observed smallness |θ_QCD| < 10⁻¹⁰ requires either fine-tuning or an additional mechanism — the Peccei-Quinn symmetry with its associated axion, which has not been observed despite fifty years of search. In ℒ_McG, Proposition VI.2’s parity-even kinetic-term requirement excludes the F·F̃ term at the Lagrangian level, and [MG-Broken, §X] supplies the reason: x₄’s expansion acts symmetrically on the three spatial dimensions, so no geometric mechanism exists to generate a strong CP-violating phase in the first place. θ = 0 is forced, not tuned. The strong CP problem does not arise.

### VIII.21.7 Dark Matter Phenomenology Is Geometric

The Standard Model requires an additional particle species — cold dark matter — to explain galactic rotation curves, the Tully-Fisher relation, and enhanced gravitational lensing. Searches for that particle species across fifty years of direct-detection and collider experiments have not found it. ℒ_McG identifies dark matter as geometric mis-accounting: the observed gravitational effects attributed to dark matter arise from the stretching of three-dimensional space by x₄’s expansion in the galactic regime, reproducing flat rotation curves, the Tully-Fisher relation, and the lensing enhancement without requiring dark matter particles. The null results of direct-detection experiments are consistent with and predicted by this identification.

### VIII.21.8 The Cosmological Constant Is Derived

In the Standard Model plus Einstein-Hilbert, Λ is a free parameter. Its observed value Λ ≈ 10⁻⁵² m⁻² is sixty to one hundred twenty orders of magnitude smaller than naive quantum-field-theoretic vacuum-energy estimates, constituting the cosmological constant problem — arguably the most severe fine-tuning problem in physics. ℒ_McG derives Λ geometrically through the McGucken horizon structure: Λ ∼ 1/R₄² with R₄ the McGucken radius at the asymptotic de Sitter horizon, and the dark-energy equation of state w_eff(z) = −1 + Ω_m(z)/(6π) is derived with no adjustable parameters. The cosmological constant problem does not arise as a fine-tuning problem because Λ is not a free parameter.

### VIII.21.9 The Horizon, Flatness, and Homogeneity Problems Are Resolved Without Inflation

The Standard Big Bang model plus the Standard Model of particle physics requires inflation to explain why the cosmic microwave background is homogeneous across causally disconnected regions, why spatial curvature is near zero, and why magnetic monopoles are absent. Inflation requires an inflaton field, a potential with specific slow-roll properties, a mechanism for ending inflation (reheating), and a specific initial condition for the inflaton. None of these is directly observed; the inflaton has not been identified with any Standard Model field, and the specific inflationary potential is chosen to match CMB observations rather than derived. ℒ_McG resolves all four problems geometrically. Every point of the CMB’s last scattering surface has been in causal contact with every other point along x₄, even when spatially disconnected, because x₄’s expansion at rate c from every event connects all points in the fourth dimension at every instant — so CMB homogeneity is automatic. Spatial flatness is the consequence of x₄’s expansion at the fixed rate ic without curvature induced by x₄ itself. Monopoles are absent because the relevant topological spaces (H²(ℝ³) = 0, π₂(S³) = 0) forbid them, not because they were inflated away. The low-entropy initial condition is the natural starting point of x₄’s directional expansion, not an unexplained boundary condition of cosmology.

### VIII.21.10 The De Broglie Clock Is Physical

In the Standard Model, the rest-mass phase factor exp(−imc²τ/ℏ) appearing in every fermion wave function is treated as a global phase without direct physical significance. De Broglie’s 1924 conjecture that every massive particle carries an internal clock at frequency ν₀ = mc²/h — directly confirmed by the Catillon 2008 channeling experiment at 80 MeV — is inherited as a notational feature of relativistic quantum mechanics without physical mechanism. In ℒ_McG, this is the matter orientation condition (M), forced by Proposition V.1 of the Dirac sector: Ψ(x, x₄) = Ψ₀(x)·exp(+I·k_C·x₄) with k_C = mc/ℏ the Compton wavenumber. The physics is that matter’s Compton oscillation is its physical coupling to x₄’s advance at the Compton rate, with the positive sign of k_C selecting matter from antimatter, the single-sided bivector action picking out a preferred side of the Clifford algebra, and the pseudoscalar I = γ⁰γ¹γ²γ³ tying the oscillation to four-dimensional Clifford geometry. De Broglie’s clock receives its answer as the rest-frame oscillation whose Lorentz-boosted spatial projection is the matter wave of Davisson-Germer-confirmed diffraction.

### VIII.21.11 The Wick Rotation and Euclidean Field Theory Acquire Physical Meaning

In the Standard Model plus lattice QCD, the Wick rotation t → −iτ is a formal analytic-continuation device without physical meaning. Every lattice QCD calculation — which is every numerical computation of hadron masses, the QCD phase diagram, and the properties of the quark-gluon plasma — proceeds through Wick rotation. Every Euclidean field theory result — the Matsubara formalism for finite-temperature QFT, the Osterwalder-Schrader reconstruction theorem, Hawking’s derivation of black hole temperature, the instanton calculus, the Hartle-Hawking no-boundary proposal — relies on a substitution whose physical meaning has remained obscure for seventy-two years. In ℒ_McG via [MG-Wick], the Wick rotation is the coordinate identification τ = x₄/c under the McGucken Principle with x₄ = ict — the physical-geometric rotation from the ordinary time axis to the fourth axis x₄. The substitution is the projection of physics onto the physical fourth axis. Every major application — path-integral convergence, Matsubara temperature, Hawking temperature, Osterwalder-Schrader reconstruction, contour rotation, instanton calculus — follows as a theorem of this identification.

### VIII.21.12 The Fundamental Constants Are Derived

In the Standard Model, c and ℏ are fundamental constants — measured empirically, treated as inputs to the theory, not derived. Their specific numerical values are historical accidents of unit choice combined with empirical measurement. In ℒ_McG via [MG-Constants], both constants follow from dx₄/dt = ic: c is the rate of x₄’s advance, and ℏ is the action per oscillatory step of that advance at the Planck frequency, with the self-consistency condition ℓ_P = √(ℏG/c³) relating all three scales. The Lindgren-Liukkonen 2019 derivation via stochastic optimization in Minkowski spacetime converges on the same identification independently, providing cross-validation. The constants are features of x₄’s advance, not empirical inputs.

### VIII.21.13 Testable Empirical Differences

ℒ_McG is not just a reframing. Several specific empirical signatures distinguish it from the Standard Model at the level of measurement. The Compton coupling of [MG-Compton] predicts a mass-independent zero-temperature residual diffusion D_x^(McG) = ε²c²Ω/(2γ²) for cold-atom and trapped-ion systems — three experimental channels, distinguishable from Adler’s CSL-type modifications of the Schrödinger equation by its zero-temperature persistence and mass independence. The cosmological-holography prediction ρ²(t_rec) ≈ 7 at recombination distinguishes McGucken-horizon entropy from Hubble-horizon entropy, distinguishable in principle through CMB structure, with the specific observational translation flagged as ongoing work in [MG-FRW-Holography, §10.5]. The geometric CP³ interpretation of the McGucken-Woit synthesis gives a testable constraint on the Higgs self-coupling λ distinguishable from the Standard Model’s free-parameter fit. The no-extra-dimensions theorem of [MG-Witten1995] predicts the absolute absence of experimentally detectable spatial dimensions beyond x₄ at any energy scale — consistent with but stronger than the null results of LEP, Tevatron, LHC, and cosmic-ray extra-dimension searches. The dark-energy equation of state w_eff(z) = −1 + Ω_m(z)/(6π) gives a specific prediction distinguishable from ΛCDM by upcoming high-precision measurements from DESI, Euclid, and the Vera Rubin Observatory.

### VIII.21.14 What the Comparison Establishes

The Standard Model is a precise empirical description of the non-gravitational sector of physics, which was largely built empirically. ℒ_McG, which arose from the physical principle dx₄/dt = ic, offers a physically deeper and broader account: it derives the Standard Model’s form from the McGucken Principle and simultaneously contains gravity, cosmology, quantum foundations, thermodynamics, and the arrows of time as consequences of the same principle — domains the Standard Model does not address at all. Within its overlap with the Standard Model, the Standard Model’s empirics — the percent-level predictions for collider physics, the flavor-physics matches, the electroweak precision tests, the Higgs mass and couplings — is preserved in ℒ_McG because the four-fold uniqueness theorem forces the same form that experiment has confirmed. In particular, no prior Lagrangian in the 282-year history of Lagrangian physics — not the Standard Model, not Einstein-Hilbert, not any combination of them — accounts for the Second Law of Thermodynamics, Brownian motion, or the arrows of time. These are not sectors of ℒ_SM+EH at all. In ℒ_McG they are theorems of the same geometric principle dx₄/dt = ic that forces the Lagrangian’s functional form (§VIII.14). What the Standard Model does not do, and ℒ_McG does, is derive that form from a single principle rather than assemble it from empirical inputs; derive the quantum structure rather than inherit it; contain gravity as one of four sectors rather than bolting it on as a separate classical theory; unify the twelve insertions of i, the universal ℏ, and the invariant c as three aspects of one dynamical postulate; unify seven arrows of time as projections of one directional fact; dissolve the strong CP problem; explain dark matter phenomenology geometrically; derive the cosmological constant; resolve the horizon, flatness, and homogeneity problems without inflation; identify the de Broglie clock as physical coupling rather than notational feature; give the Wick rotation physical meaning; derive c and ℏ rather than measure them; and supply specific empirical signatures that distinguish the framework from the Standard Model at the level of measurement.

Thirteen axes, one new principle. The Standard Model remains the working empirical theory of non-gravitational physics; ℒ_McG is the framework that identifies why the Standard Model has the form it does and connects it to gravity, cosmology, and the structures of quantum mechanics through one geometric principle. The two frameworks are complementary in the specific sense that the Standard Model’s form is what ℒ_McG’s four-fold uniqueness theorem forces, and the Standard Model’s empirics is the content that ℒ_McG inherits by reproducing that form.

## VIII.22 How the McGucken Principle Completes the Assumptions of Physics Programme: The Action Principle as (DR) + (IND) + (KE), the Canonical One-Form as the Differential of x₄-Advance, and the Physical Source at Every Terminus the Programme Reaches

Everything below descends from the physical expansion of x₄. The McGucken Principle states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the rate c from every point of spacetime, dx₄/dt = ic. Sections VIII.5–VIII.21 established thirteen results in which a phenomenon treated by the received formalism as a postulate is a theorem of that expansion. The present section addresses a body of work that arrives at the same phenomena from the opposite direction and stops one step short of the same conclusion: the Assumptions of Physics programme of Carcassi, Aidala and collaborators, whose Reverse Physics line takes a physical law as given and finds the minimal physical assumptions sufficient to recover it.

The programme bears on this paper more directly than any other framework surveyed in §VIII.4, because its central result is about the action principle itself. Carcassi and Aidala establish that the principle of stationary action is logically equivalent to three physical assumptions [Carcassi-Aidala 2023]. A paper whose thesis is that the Lagrangian of physics is forced by dx₄/dt = ic owes an account of a theorem stating that the least-action principle is equivalent to something else entirely. This section gives that account, and the account is one of completion rather than of rivalry.

The relation follows from a feature of the programme's own design. Reverse Physics is meta-theoretic by construction: it maps the logical dependencies among the conditions of the received formalism and declines, as a matter of stated method, to name a physical primitive beneath them — its regress stops, in its authors' words, at experimental controllability rather than at ontological constituents. That is a coherent and deliberate limit, and it produces one recurring pattern across the programme's results. Each derivation terminates in a quantity the derivation requires and cannot source. The third-law argument terminates in a phase-space area h that its author flags as introduced ad hoc, noting that thermodynamics establishes that a scale must exist but not what sets it. The action-principle theorem terminates in a manifold that already carries a time coordinate, with the flow's rate in that coordinate set to unity by fiat. The relativistic material terminates in a metric taken as given, its signature underived. The Reverse Quantum Mechanics analysis terminates in a Born rule that must be added to the ensemble structure as a separate assumption. In every case the terminus is correctly located by the programme and correctly left open by it.

What dx₄/dt = ic supplies is the source at each terminus, and it supplies them from one statement rather than from a list. The remainder of this section works through them.

### VIII.22.1 The action principle as (DR) + (IND) + (KE), and the slot where dx₄/dt = ic lives

Everything below descends from the physical expansion of x₄. Because the advance proceeds at rate ic from every event, any formalism describing evolution carries a rate somewhere in its statement, and the place it carries that rate is the place at which the Principle enters.

The setting of [Carcassi-Aidala 2023] is extended phase space, charted by (q^i, p_i, t), of dimension 2N + 1. On it a displacement vector field S — the vector field of the dynamical system — is constructed, whose time component is fixed by their equation (3) to the value 1. Three assumptions are then stated:

(DR) determinism and reversibility, expressed as ∇·S = 0: as many states flow into any closed region as flow out, so that no states are created or lost;

(IND) independence of the degrees of freedom, carried by a closed antisymmetric two-form ω — the state-counting form — satisfying ∂_{q^i} ∧ ω = 0 and ∂_{p_i} ∧ ω = 0, with ω = −dθ locally and ι_S ω = 0;

(KE) kinematic equivalence, p = p(q, q̇, t): the trajectory in configuration space suffices to reconstruct the dynamical state.

Their result is the equivalence (DR) + (IND) + (KE) ⟺ the principle of stationary action, with a geometric reading of the variation: δS is the flux of the displacement field through the surface spanned by a path and its variation, and it vanishes exactly when the path is a field line, so that no evolutions cross the surface. They conclude that the action and the Lagrangian are gauge-dependent and are therefore not the physical objects — the physical objects being the displacement field and the counting form — and that the action principle "is better understood as a feature of Hamiltonian mechanics, instead of Lagrangian mechanics," since it holds on every Hamiltonian system whether or not a Lagrangian exists and fails for Lagrangians that are not hyperregular.

*Where the Principle enters.* Their coordinate t carries two roles at once. It is a coordinate of the manifold, and it is the parameter of the flow. Equation (3) welds the two together by setting the flow's advance in the t-direction to 1, so that in their variables the constraint reads dt/dt = 1. That slot — the relation between the advancing direction and the parameter measuring the advance — is the slot the McGucken Principle occupies, and the Principle puts ic in it. In the framework of this paper t is not a dimension. The dimension is x₄; t is the parameter measuring how far x₄ has advanced; and the relation between them is a physical rate carrying a magnitude c and a perpendicularity marker i, rather than a dimensionless unit.

Three consequences of that difference are visible in their construction, and each marks a place where it reaches its natural limit.

*No metric.* With ic in the slot, substituting x₄ = ict into the Euclidean four-distance gives ds² = dx₁² + dx₂² + dx₃² − c²dt² directly (Proposition III.1): the Lorentzian signature is an output. With 1 in the slot one obtains a contact and symplectic structure and no interval. Their construction is metric-free as a consequence of what stands in the slot.

*No norm on the canonical one-form.* They observe that their expression (19), θ_t = −H, giving θ = p_i dq^i − H dt with components (p₁, p₂, p₃, −H), is "remarkably similar to that of the relativistic four-momentum," and leave the observation as a similarity. The four-momentum carries a norm, p^μp_μ = −m²c², which is the four-speed budget of Proposition III.2 — the covariant expression of dx₄/dt = ic. A framework with no rate and no signature has no object that could carry that norm, so the resemblance is as far as their construction reaches. Section VIII.22.2 states what the resemblance is.

*Reversibility at no cost.* On a manifold carrying t as a coordinate, running the flow backwards costs nothing, and (DR) can be assumed without appearing to assume much. In this framework there is no t-axis along which to run backwards; there is x₄ advancing in the direction +ic and never −ic, and the arrow is the theorem of §VIII.14. Their (DR) holds the magnitude content of the advance — the conserved state count follows from |dx₄/dt| = c — and is silent on its directional content. This is precisely the division stated in §VIII.16.5: the symmetry content of the Principle, processed through Noether's theorem, yields the time-symmetric conservation catalog, and the directional content yields the Second Law and the arrows. Carcassi and Aidala have the first half and assume the second.

*The three assumptions read through the Principle.* Each of the three is, in this framework, a consequence rather than a starting point.

(KE) is the single-valuedness of the x₄-advance field. Section IV.4 defines X as the accumulated magnitude of x₄'s advance from a fixed initial hypersurface, with X = cτ along each integral curve, and establishes ∂_μX ∂^μX = −1 (Proposition IV.2) as the field form of the four-speed budget. The statement that the trajectory determines the state is the statement that the momentum covector p_μ = ∂_μS is a well-defined field, which holds exactly when S, and hence X, is single-valued. Section IV.4.4 supplies both the mechanism and the failure locus: X is single-valued wherever the congruence of advance is free of caustics, and at a caustic — where neighbouring integral curves cross and the expansion scalar diverges — the accumulated advance takes more than one value at the same event and (KE) fails. The framework therefore predicts where their assumption holds, a question their formulation, taking (KE) globally, does not raise.

(DR) divides as above: the conserved count is the magnitude of the advance, the reversibility an artifact of treating t as a coordinate.

(IND) is the assumption bearing their two-form, and it carries the deepest reading here. Their ω is a two-form because degrees of freedom come in conjugate pairs, a quantity together with its conjugate, and the parallelogram spanned by two vectors is the bi-dimensional object over which states are counted. That conjugate pairing is what §VIII.6 traces to the perpendicularity of x₄: the commutator [q̂, p̂] = iℏ is the phase-space expression of x₄'s orthogonality to the three spatial dimensions, with the i marking the perpendicularity and the ℏ the action per oscillation cycle. The antisymmetry of the classical counting form and the i of the quantum commutator are then one geometric fact at two levels of description, and the framework predicts that the classical ω and the quantum [q̂, p̂] have one source. The programme's own record poses this as an open question and does not answer it (§VIII.22.4 below).

*Scope.* Their theorem is a valid equivalence and this paper does not dispute it. What is claimed is that the equivalence is conditional on being handed a manifold that already carries t, and that on this framework's premises t exists because x₄ advances: a clock reads accumulated x₄-advance, X = cτ, so with no advance there is no accumulation and nothing for a clock to register. Their result describes what holds once time is available; the Principle is what makes it available. The absence of any mention of a fourth dimension in their derivation is a fact about its vocabulary rather than about its dependencies, in the same way that Lagrange's *Mécanique Analytique* mentions no spacetime metric without thereby showing relativity dispensable to mechanics.

### VIII.22.2 The canonical one-form as the differential of x₄-advance

Everything below descends from the physical expansion of x₄. The advance accumulates along every worldline, and the accumulated advance is the object of which the canonical one-form of Hamiltonian mechanics is the differential.

Let X be the x₄-advance field of Definition IV.4.1 and let S = −mcX. The four-speed budget gives ∂_μX ∂^μX = −1, and multiplying by m²c² turns this into

g^μν ∂_μS ∂_νS = −m²c²,

the mass-shell relation with p_μ = ∂_μS — the relativistic Hamilton–Jacobi equation, and the identity recorded at Remark IV.4: Hamilton's principal function is −mc times the accumulated advance of the fourth dimension, and the mass-shell condition and the four-speed budget are one equation related by the constant −mc.

Their canonical one-form is θ = p_i dq^i − H dt, which is p_μ dx^μ with p_0 = −H/c and x⁰ = ct. Substituting p_μ = ∂_μS = −mc ∂_μX gives

θ|_L = −mc dX,

where L is the Lagrangian submanifold of their extended phase space defined by a solution of the Hamilton–Jacobi equation, the graph of p = ∂S/∂q. The restriction to L is required and does not weaken the statement. On the full extended phase space the momenta are independent coordinates and dθ = −ω ≠ 0; on L the momenta are determined by position, ω pulls back to zero, and θ becomes exact with potential −mcX. The vanishing of ω on L is the classical integrability condition permitting X to exist, which is (KE) again, and the locus where L ceases to project injectively to configuration space is the caustic locus of §IV.4.4 where X ceases to be single-valued. The three statements — θ|_L = −mc dX, ω|_L = 0, and (KE) — are one statement in three registers.

The reading this yields for their observation is direct. Their θ carries the index shape of the four-momentum, with energy in the time slot bearing a minus sign, because that slot is the x₄ slot and the minus sign is the signature that x₄ = ict produces on substitution (Proposition III.1). Along any solution the covector they wrote down is the differential of accumulated x₄-advance, and the four-momentum they noticed it resembles is what it becomes when the rate in the slot is ic rather than 1. The resemblance they record is a pullback identity, and the reason they could record it without deriving it is that their manifold carries no metric with which to complete the identification.

*Scope.* The identity holds on the Lagrangian submanifold of a Hamilton–Jacobi solution and on the caustic-free region where that submanifold projects injectively. It is not claimed that θ is exact on the full extended phase space, where it is not, nor that this framework reproduces their construction on the whole (2N + 1)-dimensional manifold.

### VIII.22.3 The metric, the signature, and the two metrics of the programme

Everything below descends from the physical expansion of x₄. The Lorentzian metric is the substitution x₄ = ict carried into the Euclidean four-distance, so the signature is an output of the Principle rather than an input to it.

The programme takes the spacetime metric as given. In its relativistic material the metric tensor appears as a supplied structure with its signature underived, and the extension to relativistic particle mechanics is a recovery within an already-Lorentzian setting. For general relativity the programme offers a conjecture and labels it one: that general relativity is equivalent to determinism and reversibility together with degree-of-freedom independence for infinitely many dense degrees of freedom. The conjecture is unproven on their side. In this paper the metric is Proposition III.1, the four-speed budget Proposition III.2, and the Einstein-Hilbert sector Proposition VI.3 by Schuller closure, with the Jacobson equation-of-state route of Remark VI.3.1 as a second convergent derivation. What the programme conjectures at the level of assumption-counting, the framework derives at the level of the geometry.

*Two different metrics, and the equivocation to avoid.* The programme does derive a metric, and precision about which one matters. In its Physical Mathematics line the strict concavity of entropy makes the Hessian negative definite and thereby recovers the Fisher–Rao information metric on the space of ensembles. That is a metric on probability distributions. The spacetime metric is a different object on a different space, and the programme takes the second as given while deriving the first. The position of this framework is that both descend from the Sphere: the Fisher–Rao metric is the statistical face of the same SO(3)-invariant measure whose probability face is the Born rule of §VIII.8, along the Fubini-Study to Fisher–Rao identification of the Wootters lineage, and the Lorentzian metric is the coordinate integral of the same advance. Where the programme has one derived metric on ensemble space and one assumed metric on spacetime, the framework has one measure with two faces.

### VIII.22.4 The origin of non-commutativity: the programme's open question

Everything below descends from the physical expansion of x₄. The commutator [q̂, p̂] = iℏ is the phase-space expression of the advance's perpendicularity, with i the perpendicularity marker and ℏ the action per Compton cycle.

Among the open problems the programme records for its Physical Mathematics line is the construction of a generalized Poisson structure on ensemble spaces, and within it the questions, in its own words: "Can we define non-commutativity of variables based on non-commutativity of composed transformations?"; "Can it be defined in terms of the generator of the composed transformation?"; and "What new axiom is needed (if at all)?" The last is the decisive one. The programme asks whether an additional axiom is required to obtain non-commutativity, and does not answer.

The answer of this framework is that no additional axiom is required, because the non-commutativity is the perpendicularity of x₄ read in the conjugate plane. Section VIII.6 derives [q̂, p̂] = iℏ from dx₄/dt = ic by two independent routes — the operator route through Stone's theorem on the Minkowski geometry, and the path-integral route through Huygens' principle and the accumulated x₄-phase — with the same i and the same ℏ reached by both, and closes the alternatives by the Stone-von Neumann argument. The programme's question asks for the generator of a composed transformation whose failure to commute would supply the algebra; here that generator is the advance itself, and the failure to commute is that advancing along x₄ and translating in the conjugate direction are motions in perpendicular planes.

The connection to §VIII.22.1 completes the reading. Their classical counting two-form is antisymmetric because degrees of freedom pair a quantity with its conjugate; their quantum question asks where the non-commutativity of that pairing comes from. Both are the same pairing, and the framework identifies its source once, at Remark III.5.2: the perpendicularity of the fourth axis, which is also the source of the twelve appearances of i catalogued there.

### VIII.22.5 The third law, the scale h, and the classical uncertainty bound

Everything below descends from the physical expansion of x₄. The advance carries one quantum of action ℏ per Compton cycle, and its resolution bottoms out at a single cycle.

The programme derives a classical uncertainty principle from the third law of thermodynamics in four steps. Step one, dimensional consistency: S = ln(Δx Δp) takes the logarithm of a dimensioned quantity, and the repair introduces a constant h of action units, S = ln(Δx Δp / h), so that a uniform distribution over a region of area h has zero entropy. Step two, the Gaussian bound: at fixed uncertainty the entropy is bounded above by S ≤ ln(2πe σ_x σ_p / h), which rearranges to σ_x σ_p ≥ (ℏ/e)e^S. Step three, the third law: S ≥ 0. Step four: with e⁰ = 1 the classical bound is σ_x σ_p ≥ ℏ/e ≈ 0.368ℏ, tightening to the quantum ℏ/2 once pure states reach zero entropy. Its author notes that he has not found this classical bound in the literature, and flags that the scale h is introduced ad hoc: thermodynamics establishes that a scale must exist, not what sets it.

The framework supplies the scale, and the derivation is Theorem 22.5.3 of [MG-Thermo], with the reading developed in §10.12quindecies of [MG-MinkHilb]. Under Clause III.3.P the advance of x₄ is oscillatory with one quantum of action per cycle, and under Postulate III.4.P a particle of rest energy mc² couples at the Compton frequency ω_C = mc²/ℏ, so the Compton period is T_C = h/(mc²) and the action per cycle is E T_C = h. The phase-space area that their dimensional argument requires is the area of one wavefront cycle. The third law they impose as an axiom then holds for a physical reason: the entropy is the logarithm of the count of x₄-advance states, that count cannot fall below one cycle, and S ≥ 0 states that the wavefront cannot be resolved more finely than a single Compton cycle. Going below S = 0 would mean resolving below one x₄-cycle, which is below the physical resolution of the advance rather than merely below an axiomatic floor.

The ratio between the two bounds acquires a mechanism by the same route. The classical ℏ/e and quantum ℏ/2 differ by 2/e ≈ 0.74, which their derivation obtains on general grounds without physical commitment. Here the classical bound corresponds to one cycle occupied independently in position and in momentum — a rectangular phase-space cell — and the quantum bound to one cycle occupied jointly, with the non-factorising occupation forced because the SO(3)-invariant measure on rays does not decompose into independent position and momentum measures. The factor 2/e is the ratio of the two cycle-occupation geometries.

*Relation to §VIII.11.* Section VIII.11 derives ΔxΔp ≥ ℏ/2 by the Fourier conjugacy of the position and momentum projections of the McGucken phase. The present route derives a classical bound from an entropy floor. These are two readings of one floor rather than two competing derivations: the entropy floor and the uncertainty floor are the same one-cycle resolution limit of the advance, read as a bound on state count and as a bound on phase-space area respectively. This is why the programme finds that each of the two forces the other — they are one floor expressed in two languages, and their mutual entailment is the signature of a common origin, which the framework names.

### VIII.22.6 The field-theory extension: an open call, and what §IV.4 supplies

Everything below descends from the physical expansion of x₄. The advance proceeds from every event of the manifold, so the object it defines is a field on spacetime rather than a flow on a finite-dimensional phase space, and the extension the programme seeks is the setting in which the Principle is naturally stated.

The programme's open-problems record for Reverse Physics is explicit. It is "actively looking for someone to help generalize Reverse Physics to classical field theory (from finitely many DOFs to continuously many DOFs)"; it asks to "find a suitable Hamiltonian/symplectic formulation of field theory (starting with electromagnetism)"; and it asks to "understand how to generalize count of states/entropy … to field theory." The obstruction is visible in the construction of §VIII.22.1: extended phase space is (2N + 1)-dimensional for N degrees of freedom, and both the counting form and the divergence-free displacement field are objects on that finite-dimensional manifold. Passing to continuously many degrees of freedom requires a count of states surviving the limit, and the programme records that it does not yet have one.

Section IV.4 of this paper is the field-theoretic setting they describe, reached from the Principle rather than by taking a limit. The x₄-advance field X carries the constraint ∂_μX ∂^μX = −1 enforced by a multiplier ρ, and the three field equations of Proposition IV.3 are the constraint, the continuity equation ∇_μ(ρ u^μ) = 0 with u^μ = −c ∂^μX, and the stress-energy tensor T_μν = ρ u_μu_ν. Their ∇·S = 0 on (q, p, t) and this ∇_μ(ρ u^μ) = 0 on spacetime are the same statement at two levels, and under their own (KE) the phase-space flow projects injectively onto the spacetime congruence, which is why the two agree and where they part company — at caustics. The multiplier is the rest-mass density of the congruence, so what their construction carries as an auxiliary variable is here a physical quantity.

For their third request the framework supplies the count directly. The entropy is the logarithm of the count of x₄-advance states in units of the action per Compton cycle. That count is defined at every event, because the advance proceeds from every event; it is finite per unit area because it is floored at one cycle (§VIII.22.5); and it is the same count whose horizon reading is the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy of §VIII.4(g) and whose event reading is the Born measure of §VIII.8. A state count native to spacetime, floored, and reproducing both endpoints is the object their third request describes. Its explicit measure-theoretic form is the hybrid measure ℳ = ℝ³ × (ℓ_P · ℤ) of §VIII.25.4, continuous in the three spatial directions and discrete in the x₄ fibre over each event, with the conjugate momentum in the fibre direction confined to a compact Brillouin zone.

*Scope.* This is offered as a correspondence and a starting point rather than as a completed generalization of their programme. What the framework supplies without qualification is the field-theoretic state count their third request names, together with the field form of the free sector in §IV.4. A symplectic formulation of field theory in their sense, and electromagnetism treated in that language, are not established here; the gauge sector of this paper is derived through Proposition VI.2 rather than reformulated symplectically, and the input carried by the hybrid measure is stated at §VIII.25.6. The claim is that the obstruction their record identifies — a count of states surviving the passage to continuously many degrees of freedom — is removed when the count is taken on the expanding wavefront rather than on phase space.

### VIII.22.8 What the completion claims, what it does not, and the invitation to challenge

Everything below descends from the physical expansion of x₄. The claim of this section is specific and its boundaries are stated directly.

*The claim.* The Assumptions of Physics programme identifies, by its own analysis and with precision, a set of places at which the received formalism rests on a scale, an axiom, or an assumption that the analysis cannot source. Those places are the programme's results rather than its failures: locating them is what the method is for. The McGucken Principle supplies a physical object at each of them, and supplies them from one statement rather than from a list. Where the programme finds that the Born rule and the von Neumann entropy and the third law and the uncertainty bound and the classical limit are one condition seen in different lights, the framework identifies the object whose faces they are — the expanding wavefront of x₄ and its invariant measure — and derives each face from it. That the several conditions converge is itself the evidence: a convergence of that kind is what a single physical source looks like when it is read through a formalism that does not name it.

*What is not claimed.* This paper does not dispute any of the programme's equivalences, and does not claim that its own derivations render them redundant; the equivalences are results about the logical architecture of the received formalism and remain results whatever lies beneath. It is not claimed that dx₄/dt = ic is necessary for a stationary-action principle in the abstract, since the programme exhibits a sufficient alternative set of assumptions on a metric-free manifold. What is claimed is that their manifold carries a time coordinate whose existence the framework sources, and that the specific functional of Proposition IV.1 requires the rate and the signature their construction lacks. The identity of §VIII.22.2 is a pullback statement on a Lagrangian submanifold and is not an equality of one-forms on the full extended phase space. The field-theory correspondence of §VIII.22.6 supplies the state count and the field form of the free sector without completing the symplectic formulation. And the programme's Physical Mathematics line — deriving topology and σ-algebras from experimental verifiability, and universal linear, geometric and measure structures from reproducibility and ensembles — addresses the mathematics of experimental science rather than a physical primitive; this framework neither competes with it nor completes it, and the entropic-geometry results of that line are the point at which the two touch.

*Evidence levels.* The action-principle theorem, the classical limit, the unphysicality argument and the Reverse Quantum Mechanics independence result are drawn from published papers and preprints and are engaged on their text. The open-problems record, the statements about the metric in the relativistic material, and the four-step third-law argument are drawn from the programme's lecture and summary material and are engaged as its stated positions rather than as journal results; quotations above are from that material.

*Invitation to challenge.* As with the claims of §§VIII.5–VIII.18, this one is stated with the expectation that it can and should be examined. The claim is falsified in either of two ways. If any terminus listed in §VIII.22.7 is shown to be sourced within the programme's own resources — the scale h fixed by its thermodynamics rather than introduced, the signature derived rather than supplied, the non-commutativity obtained without an added axiom — then that row is not a completion and should be struck. If a framework other than this one is shown to supply a physical source at every one of those termini from a single statement, the claim of this section would pass to that framework. No such framework has been identified in the survey conducted here.

*The relation, stated once.* Reverse Physics finds that the conditions of the received formalism are one condition in different lights, and stops there by method. The McGucken Principle names what they are conditions of. Each terminus the programme reaches with care is a place where the analysis has run to the edge of the formalism and found that something further is required — a scale, a floor, an axiom, a rate — and each is a place where dx₄/dt = ic says what that something is. The programme's rigor in locating the termini is what makes the completion legible; without its map of where the formalism runs out, a physical source at each point would have nowhere to land.


### VIII.22.9 The programme classified by channel

The separation of §III.8 supplies a compact statement of what the Assumptions of Physics programme carries and what it does not, and the statement explains the pattern of termini catalogued in §VIII.22.7 rather than merely listing them.

The programme is Channel A throughout. Its objects are an extended phase space, a state count, a two-form, a flow, and a set of assumptions about determinism, reversibility, independence and kinematic equivalence. Every one of these is an algebraic or measure-theoretic condition on a space of states. None of them is a statement that anything propagates: there is no rate in the construction until one is put in by hand at the programme's equation (3), where the flow's temporal component is set to unity, and there is no wavefront anywhere in it. This is not a defect of execution but a consequence of method — Reverse Physics seeks the minimal assumptions sufficient to recover a formalism, and a propagation content is not minimal for that purpose, because the formalism can be recovered without one.

The classification predicts where the programme's results stop. A Channel A framework can reach what algebra and invariance can reach: it can prove the action principle equivalent to its three assumptions, it can identify the canonical one-form and observe its resemblance to a four-momentum, it can derive the entropy bounds of its third-law argument up to a scale, and it can prove the independence of the Born rule from the rest of its structure. What it cannot reach is a rate, a signature, a causal cone, or a physical scale, because each of those is a propagation fact rather than a symmetry fact. Every open terminus in the ledger of §VIII.22.7 is of that kind: the rate set to unity by fiat, the metric and its signature taken as given, the scale h introduced ad hoc, the floor imposed as an axiom, the state count sought for field theory. The programme runs out precisely where Channel B content would be required, and it runs out nowhere else.

What the Principle supplies at each terminus is therefore the missing channel, not a further assumption of the same kind. The rate ic in the slot the programme leaves at unity, the signature by substitution of x₄ = ict, the identification θ|_L = −mc dX of the canonical one-form as the differential of accumulated advance, the scale h as action per Compton cycle, the causal cone that Schuller closure takes as its input, and the state count defined at every event — these are Channel B facts, and they are available because the Principle carries both channels in one statement rather than one channel in an assumption ledger. Section VIII.23 develops the general form of that claim: the presence of two channels in one geometric-dynamical statement is what makes overdetermination possible, and overdetermination is what distinguishes a foundation from a well-executed axiomatization of an existing formalism.

### VIII.22.10 The gauge hidden in θ, and why every Lagrangian carries it

Everything below descends from the physical expansion of x₄. The advance has a fixed magnitude and a fixed direction and no fixed origin, and the two consequences of that fact — one about the action, one about the gauge sector — answer a conjecture the programme states and leaves open.

#### The conjecture

The programme's derivation of the action reaches a gauge freedom and stops there. From (DR) the displacement field on extended phase space is divergence-free, hence admits a potential θ with S = −∇×θ; the gauge θ = (p, 0, −H) may be chosen "without loss of generality"; the line integral of θ along a curve is the action. The observation drawn from this is that the gauge freedom in θ is the reason Lagrangians are not unique — "if you study Lagrangian mechanics somebody might have told you that the Lagrangians are not unique for the system. This is why they're not unique. There is a gauge hidden there" (20:06) — and the conjecture drawn from that is stated in the next breath:

> "And I think one should be able to prove that all Lagrangian theory have to be gauge theory in some sense. And this is something that once we are able to expand all of this to field theory is going to be something that we are looking for." (20:06–20:22)

The remainder of the argument establishes that the variation of the action is gauge-invariant: δS is the line integral of θ around the boundary of the surface Σ between a path and its variation, hence by Stokes' theorem the flux of the state-flow through Σ, which vanishes exactly when the path is everywhere tangent to the flow.

Both halves of this have answers under the Principle, and the answers are not of the same kind: the first is an identification already recorded in §VIII.22.2, the second is a theorem already proved in §VI.2.

#### The gauge in θ is the zero-point of the advance

Section VIII.22.2 establishes θ|_L = −mc dX on the Lagrangian submanifold of a Hamilton–Jacobi solution, with X the accumulated x₄-advance of Definition IV.4.1. That identity names the gauge freedom immediately. A change θ → θ + df alters X by an additive function and leaves dX unchanged; the additive freedom in X is the choice of where the counting of the advance begins. The rate of the advance is what the Principle fixes and is what no gauge transformation touches. So the non-uniqueness of Lagrangians is the non-uniqueness of the x₄-origin, and the gauge-invariant content underneath it is dx₄/dt = ic itself.

The gauge-invariance of the variation follows in the same terms. Their state-flow S is, under the Principle, the advance: the flow of states forward in the parameter is x₄ advancing at ic from every point. Their Stokes argument then reads as a statement about that advance — δS is the flux of x₄-advance through Σ, and it vanishes exactly when the path is everywhere tangent to the advance, so that none of the advance crosses it. Stationary action is the condition that the worldline runs along the advance rather than across it. This is the same conclusion as §VIII.16.5 reached from the free sector, obtained here from their construction rather than from ℒ_free.

One point in their reading does not carry over, and the disagreement is worth stating because the corrected version is the weaker claim. They conclude from the gauge freedom that the action is "an unphysical quantity." Under the Principle it is not. In the gauge they themselves adopt without loss of generality, θ = −mc dX, so along any solution

∫_γ θ = −mc ΔX = −mc² Δτ,

which is the standard free relativistic action and is the accumulated advance measured in units of mc. What the gauge freedom removes is the origin of the count, not the quantity counted. The action is physical and has no preferred zero — the same status as a potential energy or an x₄-coordinate, and for the same reason, since it is mc times an x₄-coordinate. Their own gauge choice is the one in which this is visible.

#### Why every Lagrangian carries a gauge freedom

The conjecture that all Lagrangian theories must be gauge theories is answered under the Principle at the level of form, and the answer runs through the phase rather than through the origin.

The Principle fixes the magnitude of the advance and its direction and fixes no orthogonal reference within the plane perpendicular to it. The choice of phase reference at each spacetime point is therefore free, and free independently at each point, which is local phase invariance — established at Proposition III.5 and [MG-SM, Theorem 5]. Every Lagrangian sector of ℒ_McG is built from fields coupled to the advance, so every sector inherits that freedom. Demanding that the dynamics be insensitive to a phase reference chosen independently at each point forces a connection, and Proposition VI.2 establishes that the unique Lorentz-invariant, parity-invariant, renormalizable Lagrangian for that connection, first-order in its derivatives, is the Yang-Mills form −¼F^a_μν F^{aμν} for any compact Lie group G ([MG-SM, Theorems 10–11]).

So the two observations are the same gauge structure approached from opposite sides. Their construction finds a gauge freedom in the potential of the state-flow and cannot say what it is a freedom of, because their extended phase space carries no geometry in which a phase could be referenced. The Principle supplies the geometry, and the freedom is the phase reference in the plane perpendicular to the advance. Their conjecture that Lagrangian theory must be gauge theory is, in this framework, the statement that every functional built from an advance with no fixed phase reference carries that indeterminacy, together with the uniqueness result fixing what the resulting gauge Lagrangian must be. The field-theoretic extension they say they are looking for is where the derivation of Proposition VI.2 already operates.

#### Scope

Three limits, stated so that the claim is not read wider than it is.

*What the uniqueness result does and does not fix.* Proposition VI.2 and [MG-SM, Theorems 10–11] force the Yang-Mills form for a given compact G, and they alone do not select G. The selection is supplied separately, in [MG-QFT, §9], by the internal algebra 𝒜_F = ℂ ⊕ ℍ ⊕ M₃(ℂ) and the reading of each summand's gauge group as its projective inner-automorphism group; §VIII.25.6 states what that derivation leaves empirical. The two results are independent and their conjunction is what fixes both the group and the Lagrangian on it.

*The general theorem is not proved.* "Every Lagrangian theory is a gauge theory," quantified over all Lagrangian field theories, is not established here and is not established by them. What is established here is why the Lagrangians forced by the Principle carry a gauge freedom and what that freedom is. A theory not built from the advance is outside the argument's reach.

*Two freedoms, not one.* The free sector carries a reparametrization invariance as well as the phase freedom, and these are distinct. Reparametrization invariance holds because the advance is a geometric fact about a worldline and does not depend on the parameter used to trace it; it is fixed by the einbein elimination of Proposition IV.4. The phase freedom holds because the advance fixes no orthogonal reference; it is fixed by a choice of gauge for the connection. The additive freedom in X identified above is a third and is the global part of the phase freedom, the case f = constant. The Principle sources all three, and they should not be conflated: it is the local phase freedom, not the origin of X, that forces the gauge sector.

### VIII.22.11 The high-entropy limit, the two arenas as coarse-grainings, and the programme's closing question

Everything below descends from the physical expansion of x₄. The advance carries one quantum of action per Compton cycle, so a description resolved more coarsely than a cycle cannot register the cycle structure, and what it registers instead is a classical description. The reading given here is developed in [MG-MinkHilb, §§10.12quaterdecies–quindecies] and [MG-Thermo, Theorem 22.5]; the present section states the part that bears on the Lagrangian program and adds three items from the published paper that those treatments do not use.

#### The result and what it establishes

[Carcassi-Landini-Aidala 2026] establishes that classical mechanics is the high-entropy limit of quantum mechanics: high entropy masks quantum effects, and mixed states of sufficiently high entropy are approximated by classical distributions over phase space. The identity carrying the result is that entropy is the logarithm of the count of states measured in units of ℏ, so that the mathematical limit ℏ → 0 is the same operation as setting the entropy of pure states to −∞, and the physically meaningful form of the limit is S ≫ 0 rather than a limit taken on a constant. The mechanism is a stretching map on phase space, realized quantum-mechanically as a purely dissipative Lindblad evolution with jump operator a†, which rescales anti-normally ordered expectations by λ^(n+m) and takes [X̂, P̂] = iℏ/λ, so that λ → ∞ performs the group contraction from the Moyal bracket to the Poisson bracket.

The result is stated as independent of mechanism by design: "this limit is therefore independent of what interpretation one may give to either quantum mechanics or entropy, and from the reason, the mechanism, that makes a high-entropy description suitable," with the parallel drawn to the low-speed limit, where "no one tries to find a reason, a mechanism, as to why speeds become low in certain regimes." That is a methodological choice of the kind §VIII.24.1 records, and it is what leaves the floor being masked against unaccounted for.

#### The limit as loss of x₄-resolution, and the two arenas as its two coarse-grainings

Under the Principle the limit has a definite geometric content. Since ℏ is the action per Compton cycle of the advance, ℏ → 0 is the regime in which the cycle falls below every resolved scale, so the discrete advance is coarse-grained into a continuum and the wavefront is indistinguishable from a classical one. Their S ≫ 0 is loss of x₄-resolution.

[MG-MinkHilb, §10.12quaterdecies] carries this further, and the further step is what connects their result to the two arenas the corpus derives. Minkowski space is the coordinate integral ∫ic dt = ict, which sums over the individual cycles and delivers a continuous coordinate in which the cycle structure is no longer resolved. The Born density is the modulus-frozen reading, in which the squaring discards the phase. Each is obtained from the McGucken Sphere by summing over a distinguishable structure — the cycle count in the first case, the phase in the second — and summing over a distinguishable structure is, in their own state-counting, an increase of entropy by the logarithm of the number of collapsed alternatives. Their own two-slit illustration is the second case exactly: the maximally mixed state makes a randomized phase indistinguishable from a randomized path, so the phase degree of freedom has been summed over. The two arenas are therefore higher-entropy readings of one lower-entropy object, and the object is the expanding Sphere with its cycles and phase intact.

The scope of that identification is stated at [MG-MinkHilb, §10.12quaterdecies]: their entropy is the von Neumann entropy of mixed states, the concealment at issue is an information coarse-graining, and the two agree for these two structures because the cycle count and the phase are distinguishable degrees of freedom whose loss is an increase in state count. It is not claimed that every coarse-graining in the framework is thermodynamic entropy.

#### The floor as the condition that makes the arenas integrable

The one-cycle floor of §VIII.22.5 has a second role that bears directly on the construction of ℒ_McG. Both arenas are integrals of the advance, and an integral is a sum over increments. Were the increments unbounded below, the sum would run over a continuum of infinitesimal expansions with no smallest cell, no well-defined measure would exist, and the construction would carry the pathology that the entropy logarithm carries without its cell h — the same gap one level down. The floor closes both at once: the advance is quantized at one cycle, the sum has a smallest cell, and each integral converges to a space carrying a proper measure — Lebesgue on the spatial slice, and the SO(3)-invariant measure on rays that the Born reading requires ([MG-MinkHilb, §10.12quindecies]).

The order is logical rather than causal. All three — the entropy floor, the metric, and the Hilbert space — descend from dx₄/dt = ic, and the floor is the measure-theoretic precondition of the integration rather than a separate cause upstream of the other two. In field theory the same requirement returns as the hybrid measure of §VIII.25.4, where the discreteness in the x₄ fibre is what bounds the loop integrals; the floor that makes the entropy well-defined and the floor that makes the field-theoretic measure well-defined are the same floor.

#### Three items from the published paper

*Atomic stability read off the entropy floor.* The paper draws a consequence its earlier presentations do not: "classical states for which σ_x σ_p < ℏ/e are states with negative entropy, which imply a breakdown of thermodynamics. It is no wonder, then, electrons cannot fall on the nucleus or that classical mechanics gives us the wrong spectra for black-body radiation. Classical mechanics fails at low entropy." Under the Principle the floor whose violation this identifies is the one-cycle floor, so the stability of the atom against radiative collapse and the failure of the Rayleigh–Jeans law are two instances of the same fact: a description cannot resolve below one Compton cycle of the advance. The framework does not add a mechanism to their argument here; it names what the floor is, and the naming makes the two failures instances of one geometric limit rather than two thermodynamic coincidences.

*Spin as exempt from the limit.* "The maximum entropy attainable by a quantum system is the logarithm of the dimension of the corresponding Hilbert space, [so] the high-entropy limit exists only for infinite-dimensional spaces. This explains why spin, which lives in a finite-dimensional space, is an intrinsically quantum property." The framework reaches the same exemption from the other side. Spin is the 4π-periodicity of the McGucken-Sphere SO(3) lift on Cl(1,3)⁺ Weyl spinors (§VIII.25.2), which is a property of the geometry of the advance rather than a property of a resolution scale, so no amount of coarse-graining reaches it. Their dimensional argument and this geometric one identify the same exemption, and the geometric one says what the exempt structure is.

*The two axes of their four-quadrant figure.* The paper opens with the four-quadrant diagram whose axes are Speed and Entropy, replacing the usual size axis, and closes with the statement that ℏ → 0 should be read as S ≫ 0 in the same way that c → ∞ should be read as v ≪ c. Under the Principle the two axes are the two parameters of one advance: c is its rate and ℏ the action per cycle of it. A worldline at v ≪ c spends almost none of its four-speed budget on spatial motion (Proposition III.2); a description at S ≫ 0 is coarse compared with one cycle of the same motion. Their two limits stand in a formal parallel that their method takes as an analogy; here the parallel is expected, because the two constants bounding the diagram are two attributes of a single geometric fact (§VIII.10). Their own reading of quantization as the imposition of an entropy floor completes the picture: the ℏ-floor and the c-ceiling are the two edges of one parameter space.

#### The closing question

The paper ends with a question rather than a claim: "we wonder whether it can be proven that quantum mechanics is the only way to fix the low-entropy range of classical mechanics. That is, is quantum mechanics the only theory that can recover classical mechanics at high entropy? To us, this is a question whose answer would be interesting regardless of the outcome."

Their own §4 narrows what an answer would have to establish: "if by classical limit we mean the group contraction, then the only way to achieve it is through the rescaling of the commutator between X and P," and the Moyal bracket is the unique one-parameter Lie-algebraic deformation of the Poisson bracket. The question therefore reduces to what fixes the commutator, and the corpus answers it on two sides.

On the deformation side the answer is theirs: Moyal uniqueness says there is one way to impose the floor once the floor is to be imposed by a deformation of the bracket. On the physical side the answer is the two-route derivation of §VIII.6.7 together with the closure of §VIII.6.3: [q̂, p̂] = iℏ follows from dx₄/dt = ic along two chains that share no intermediate machinery, and the Stone–von Neumann theorem then fixes the representation up to unitary equivalence, excluding non-quantum alternatives at that step. The bracket their contraction rescales is not a free choice among candidates; it is the projection of the x₄-advance onto the conjugate plane, with its i the perpendicularity of x₄ and its ℏ the action per cycle.

*Scope of that answer.* Stone–von Neumann fixes the representation given the canonical relation, and the two-route result fixes which relation the advance produces. Together they establish that among theories imposing the low-entropy floor by non-commutativity, the bracket and its representation are determined. A theory flooring the entropy by some other means is outside what the argument excludes. Their question as put is broader than the answer available here, and the corresponding claim of [MG-MinkHilb, §10.12quindecies] — that the deformation-side and primitive-side answers agree "within their respective classes" — is the same qualification stated from the other direction.

## VIII.23 Overdetermination as the Criterion for a Foundation, and the Dual-Channel Reading at Four Levels: Formulation, Ontology, Dynamics, and Causality

Everything below descends from the physical expansion of x₄. The McGucken Principle states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the rate c from every point of spacetime, dx₄/dt = ic. Section III.8 separated the two contents carried by that statement — Channel A, the algebraic-symmetry content, and Channel B, the geometric-propagation content — and §VIII.6.7 exhibited the two disjoint derivational chains those channels support, Propositions H.1–H.5 and Propositions L.1–L.6, each terminating in the canonical commutation relation [q̂, p̂] = iℏ. The present section draws the two consequences of that architecture that bear on the standing of ℒ_McG as a whole.

The first consequence is evidentiary. When one claim follows from one principle along two chains that share no intermediate machinery, the claim is *overdetermined* relative to the principle. Section VIII.23.1 develops overdetermination as a general pattern, distinguishes it from equivalence in the sense of Feynman and Stone–von Neumann, exhibits four historical instances of the pattern, and states the three-part criterion it yields for distinguishing a foundation from a reformulation. The criterion is then applied to ℒ_McG.

The second consequence is that the dual-channel reading is not confined to the question of which formulation of quantum mechanics is primary. The same separation applies, unchanged, at three further levels of description: the ontological level, where it produces wave/particle duality (§VIII.23.2); the dynamical level, where it produces the Schrödinger and Heisenberg pictures together with a geometric proof of their equivalence (§VIII.23.3); and the causal/correlational level, where it produces the coexistence of the local operator algebra of quantum field theory with the nonlocal Bell correlations of entangled systems (§VIII.23.4). Section VIII.23.5 assembles the four levels and states what their agreement establishes. The material of this section is developed at length in the companion papers [MG-TwoRoutes], [MG-Nonlocality] and [MG-Equiv]; the present section states it in the form the Lagrangian program uses, with proofs supplied where the argument turns on them.

### VIII.23.1 The Overdetermination Principle

#### VIII.23.1.1 The pattern

A claim is *overdetermined* relative to a principle when it follows from the principle along two or more derivational chains that share no essential intermediate machinery. The pattern has exactly three components.

*A common start.* One principle serves as the point of departure for every chain. In the case at hand the common start is dx₄/dt = ic.

*A common end.* One claim serves as the destination of every chain. In the case at hand the common end is [q̂, p̂] = iℏ.

*Disjoint intermediates.* The chains must not share essential machinery. If one chain's intermediate content is contained in the other's, or if both pass through the same theorem at a corresponding step, the two are variants of a single derivation rather than independent derivations. The disjointness required is substantive, not cosmetic: different mathematical machinery, different content drawn from the starting principle, different intermediate theorems, with agreement only at the two endpoints.

All three components are present in §VIII.6.7. The start is dx₄/dt = ic; the end is [q̂, p̂] = iℏ; and the intermediate catalog compiled at the close of that section shows the two chains sharing no theorem, no channel of the Principle's content, and no mathematical apparatus. The Hamiltonian chain consumes Channel A and passes through the Minkowski metric, Stone's theorem, the configuration representation, the direct commutator computation, and the Stone–von Neumann uniqueness theorem. The Lagrangian chain consumes Channel B and passes through Huygens' principle, iterated spherical expansion, Compton-frequency phase accumulation, the Feynman kernel, and Gaussian integration. Neither chain invokes a step of the other.

#### VIII.23.1.2 Why the pattern carries weight

Three reasons, of increasing depth.

*Confirmation.* A claim established by two independent methods is confirmed twice independently. In mathematics the availability of several proofs of a theorem is not redundancy; it is evidence that the theorem is a consequence of the axioms rather than an artifact of a proof technique. The analogue in physics is exact. The commutation relation [q̂, p̂] = iℏ is not an artifact of having elected to work through Stone's theorem, nor an artifact of having elected to work through Huygens' principle. It follows through both.

*Illumination.* Different proofs of one theorem expose different aspects of it. The Hamiltonian route exhibits the commutation relation as an algebraic-symmetry consequence of translation invariance on a complex Hilbert space. The Lagrangian route exhibits the same relation as a geometric-propagation consequence of spherical expansion in spacetime. Both characterizations are correct, and each is concealed by the other in the standard presentations. Taken together the two routes show that the commutation relation is simultaneously an algebraic fact and a geometric fact. A framework reaching the relation by one route exhibits one of its two aspects.

*Discrimination between a foundation and a reformulation.* This is the reason that matters for the present paper. A reformulation of existing physics in new variables can reach known results along its own chain, but it is constrained to reach them by construction: it was engineered to reproduce them. A correct foundation generates known results as consequences, and if it carries enough content it generates the same consequence along several independent chains. When that occurs, the overdetermination is evidence that the framework was not engineered to produce the result, because engineering a result along one chain does not engineer it along a disjoint second chain. This was Einstein's own argument for special relativity: the Lorentz transformations derived from the relativity principle together with the invariance of c are the same transformations derived from the form invariance of Maxwell's equations, and the agreement of two independent starting points was his evidence that relativity is a kinematic principle applying to all physics rather than a device for saving electrodynamic phenomena.

#### VIII.23.1.3 Overdetermination is not equivalence

A confusion worth forestalling is between overdetermination in the present sense and equivalence in the sense of Feynman 1948 and Stone–von Neumann 1931–1932.

Equivalence of the Hamiltonian and Lagrangian formulations of quantum mechanics is the claim that both yield the same predictions for every measurable quantity, established by Feynman's derivation of the Schrödinger equation from the path integral and by the Stone–von Neumann uniqueness of the Schrödinger representation.
