Equivalence is bidirectional: each formulation is derivable from the other.

Overdetermination is a claim from above: one principle yields both formulations along chains that do not pass through each other. The Hamiltonian route of §VIII.6.7 does not use the path integral; the Lagrangian route does not use Stone's theorem or the direct commutator.

Equivalence is compatible with overdetermination and distinct from it. Equivalence says the two formulations describe the same physics; overdetermination says they descend independently from a common source. Equivalence is a property of quantum mechanics as it stands; overdetermination is a property of a framework that derives quantum mechanics. The distinction matters because equivalence alone identifies no foundation: Feynman's demonstration that the path integral and the operator formalism agree does not settle whether both descend from a common principle. It settles only that, given both as starting points, they describe the same physics. The question "which is primary?" is left open by equivalence theorems, and the answer supplied here is that neither is — both descend from dx₄/dt = ic as the two readings of its dual-channel content.

#### VIII.23.1.4 The pattern in the historical record

The two-route derivation is not the first overdetermination in physics. Four instances fix the pattern.

*Special relativity.* The Lorentz transformations follow from the Einstein postulates through the kinematic analysis of §§4–5 of Einstein 1905, and independently from the form invariance of Maxwell's equations under the transformations Lorentz had already identified on electrodynamic grounds. The agreement of the kinematic route with the electromagnetic route was Einstein's evidence that relativity applies to all physics rather than to electromagnetism alone.

*Noether's first theorem.* Conservation of energy follows in Hamiltonian mechanics as the Noether charge of time-translation symmetry of the Hamiltonian, and independently in Lagrangian mechanics as the Noether charge of time-translation symmetry of the Lagrangian. The overdetermination of the conservation law by the two formulations is the content of Noether's theorem as a bridge between them.

*Hawking radiation.* Hawking's 1975 derivation proceeds through a Bogoliubov-coefficient analysis of quantum field theory on a black-hole background. The independent Euclidean derivation proceeds through the observation that the Wick rotation of the black-hole metric is a smooth cap whose periodicity in imaginary time equals the inverse Hawking temperature. The two reach T_H = ℏκ/(2πck_B) through disjoint machinery, and the agreement is taken as evidence that the effect is a genuine consequence of quantum field theory on a curved background rather than an artifact of a calculational route.

*The Gauss–Bonnet theorem.* At the mathematical level, the integral of the Gaussian curvature over a closed surface equals 2π times the Euler characteristic. The result follows from local differential geometry by curvature integration and independently from algebraic topology by identification of the Euler characteristic as a topological invariant. The agreement is evidence that the theorem is a genuine bridge between curvature and topology rather than a coincidence of two computations.

In each instance the pattern — two independent chains, one start, one end — is what distinguishes a genuine consequence from an artifact of calculational choice. The two-route derivation of the canonical commutation relation from dx₄/dt = ic is the first appearance of the pattern in the foundations of quantum mechanics: the first occasion on which the canonical commutation relation has been reached from a single physical principle along two chains of disjoint machinery.

#### VIII.23.1.5 The criterion, and its application to ℒ_McG

The discussion yields a criterion for distinguishing a foundation from a reformulation or an equivalence theorem. A framework qualifies as a foundation for a body of physics if and only if:

(i) it starts from a principle that is not itself a reformulation of the physics to be derived;

(ii) it derives the known results of that physics as consequences of the principle rather than importing them as inputs; and

(iii) it exhibits overdetermination — at least one important result of that physics is reached along several disjoint chains from the principle.

ℒ_McG satisfies all three. On (i), dx₄/dt = ic is a geometric-dynamical statement about a physical fourth axis in spacetime, not a rewriting of the Lagrangian it forces. On (ii), the four sectors of Theorem VI.1 together with the Higgs sector of Theorem VI.2 are derived as consequences, with the empirical inputs of the Standard Model reduced accordingly (§VIII.21.1). On (iii), overdetermination is exhibited at four places in the present paper and its companions.

*The canonical commutation relation*, through Propositions H.1–H.5 and Propositions L.1–L.6 (§VIII.6.7).

*Huygens' principle*, through the direct spherical-expansion route of [MG-HLA] and through the retarded Green's-function route of [MG-Proof], which pass through no common theorem.

*The Second Law of Thermodynamics*, through the Brownian-motion route of [MG-Entropy], through the x₄-directionality route of [MG-Singular], and through the conservation-law and arrow-of-time route of §VIII.14. Three chains, not two.

*The Einstein–Hilbert sector*, through the Schuller closure route of Proposition VI.3 and through the Jacobson horizon-thermodynamic route of Remark VI.3.1. The former consumes the universal principal polynomial as its input; the latter consumes the horizon mode count on null hypersurfaces. Both terminate in the same action with the same coefficient c⁴/16πG.

Remark VIII.23.1 (what fails the criterion). The frameworks surveyed in [MG-TwoRoutes, §VI] fail at (i) or (iii) or both. Feynman's 1948 path integral fails (i): it is a reformulation of existing quantum mechanics, and it has no principle with respect to which overdetermination could be exhibited. Nelson's stochastic mechanics passes (i) by starting from a stochastic postulate and fails (iii): it reaches the Schrödinger equation along one route and derives neither the commutation relation nor the representation structure separately. Geometric quantization fails (i), since classical phase space is itself a rewriting of classical mechanics, and fails (iii), reaching the operator formulation along a single route. The pattern across the survey is uniform. The channel taxonomy of §III.8.3 explains why: overdetermination of the kind at issue requires two channels of content in the starting principle, and a framework carrying one channel has only one chain available to it.

### VIII.23.2 The Ontological Level: Wave and Particle

#### VIII.23.2.1 The reading

Under dx₄/dt = ic, wave/particle duality is not a puzzle requiring an interpretive scheme. It is the dual-channel content read at the ontological level — at the level of what quantum objects are.

*Channel B generates the wave aspect.* The spherical symmetry of x₄'s expansion about every spacetime point is Huygens' principle (Proposition L.1), and iterated Huygens composition (Proposition L.2) generates wavefront propagation. The interference observed in double-slit geometries is the superposition of Huygens wavelets from the two apertures; the diffraction observed in single-slit geometries is the superposition of wavelets from each point of one aperture. The matter wavelength λ_dB = h/p observed by Davisson and Germer, by Thomson, and in every subsequent matter-wave experiment is the x₄-phase accumulation of matter per unit of spatial displacement, at the Compton frequency ω_C = mc²/ℏ of Proposition L.3.

*Channel A generates the particle aspect.* The invariance of x₄'s advance under spacetime translations gives, by Stone's theorem (Proposition H.2), self-adjoint generators whose eigenvalues are the four-momenta of the theory (Proposition H.3). A detection event at a definite pixel is an eigenvalue event of the position observable. The quantized exchanges of energy and momentum observed in the photoelectric effect and in Compton scattering are eigenvalue exchanges of the Channel A observables, conserved event by event. The Heisenberg relation Δx·Δp ≥ ℏ/2, which is the quantitative form of the complementarity between the two aspects, is by [MG-Uncertainty] a theorem about the Fourier duality of the x₄-phase, whose algebraic content is Channel A and whose propagation content is Channel B.

A quantum object traversing a two-slit apparatus does both at once. Its Channel B content is the wavefront reaching both apertures and interfering at the screen. Its Channel A content is the eigenvalue event at one pixel. There is no contradiction, because the two are not competing descriptions of one thing; they are two simultaneous readings of one geometric fact, corresponding to two distinct contents present in the statement of the Principle.

#### VIII.23.2.2 The classical puzzles

*The two-slit puzzle.* Interference requires both apertures because the wavelets from both are needed for the superposition; closing one removes one family of wavelets. The pattern is lost when which-path information is obtained because a which-path measurement is an eigenvalue event of the aperture-position observable, and registering an eigenvalue is a Channel A act on a system whose interference is a Channel B phenomenon. The suppression is the consequence of having elected to read one channel at the measurement, not a philosophical difficulty requiring complementarity as a doctrine.

*The delayed-choice puzzle.* The decision to register wave or particle behavior may be taken after the object has traversed the apparatus because both readings are available at every point of the path, not manufactured retroactively by the measurement. The Channel B wavefront is present throughout the apparatus; the Channel A eigenvalue event occurs at the detector. The delayed choice is a choice of which channel to register at the detector, not an alteration of what occurred earlier.

*The quantum-eraser puzzle.* Interference is recoverable after which-path information has been obtained and erased because the two channels are not ordered in time — the eigenvalue structure and the wavefront structure coexist at every spacetime point. Obtaining and erasing which-path information are operational choices about which channel to correlate with the final detection; neither alters the dual-channel content of the Principle. The eraser experiments demonstrate the simultaneity of the two channels rather than retroactive causation.

*The matter-wave and molecular-interference puzzles.* Electrons, neutrons, atoms and molecules interfere because Channel B applies to anything coupled to x₄'s oscillation, with wavelength λ = h/(Mv) scaled by the total mass. For sufficiently low velocities the wavelength stays comparable to the grating period even for very large molecules, which is why interference has been observed for C₆₀ and C₇₀ fullerenes, for phthalocyanine, and for molecules of some thousands of atomic mass units. There is no lower bound on wave behavior imposed by a boundary between a quantum and a classical realm, because on the present account there is no such boundary: all matter carries Channel B content by virtue of its coupling to the advance of x₄.

Remark VIII.23.2 (dissolution rather than interpretation). Complementarity, pilot waves, branching, decoherence, relational states and epistemic reinterpretation each accept the dual aspect as irreducible and supply a scheme for living with it. Under the present reading the duality is derived, and derived twice over from one statement. The distinction between interpreting a duality and deriving it is the same distinction drawn in §VIII.23.1.3 between equivalence and overdetermination, applied at the ontological level.

### VIII.23.3 The Dynamical Level: The Schrödinger and Heisenberg Pictures

#### VIII.23.3.1 The reading

Quantum-mechanical time evolution admits two representations. In the Schrödinger picture, states evolve under iℏ d|ψ(t)⟩/dt = Ĥ|ψ(t)⟩ while operators are held fixed. In the Heisenberg picture, operators evolve under iℏ dÂ(t)/dt = [Â(t), Ĥ] while states are held fixed. The two are related by the unitary Û(t) = exp(−iĤt/ℏ) and agree on every expectation value.

Under dx₄/dt = ic the two pictures are the dual-channel reading at the dynamical level. Channel B, the propagation content, updates the state: the wavefront is carried forward by the advance of x₄, in the path-integral sense of Propositions L.1–L.5. Channel A, the algebraic content, updates the observable: the algebraic definition of the observable is carried into the time-translated frame, in the matrix-mechanical sense of Heisenberg's original treatment of observables as the primary dynamical objects. One physical process — the advance of x₄ by ic·t from every spacetime point — is represented in two ways, according to which mathematical object is updated to record it.

This reading supplies more than a labelling. It supplies a geometric proof of the equivalence of the two pictures, in which the equality of expectation values follows from the physical content of x₄'s advance rather than from a manipulation of the evolution operator.

#### VIII.23.3.2 The equivalence theorem

**Theorem VIII.23.1 (Schrödinger–Heisenberg equivalence from dx₄/dt = ic).** Let ℋ be the complex Hilbert space of states of a physical system, let Ĥ be the self-adjoint generator of time translations on ℋ, let Û(t) = exp(−iĤt/ℏ) be the associated one-parameter unitary group, let |ψ⟩ ∈ ℋ, and let Â = Â† be an observable on ℋ. Define the Schrödinger-picture state |ψ_S(t)⟩ = Û(t)|ψ⟩ with Â held fixed, and the Heisenberg-picture observable Â_H(t) = Û(t)†ÂÛ(t) with |ψ⟩ held fixed. Then

⟨ψ_S(t)| Â |ψ_S(t)⟩ = ⟨ψ| Â_H(t) |ψ⟩,

and under the McGucken Principle this equality holds not as an algebraic identity of unitary conjugation but as a consequence of the geometric content of dx₄/dt = ic: both sides compute the expectation of the same observable after the same physical process — the advance of x₄ by ic·t from every spacetime point — with the two sides differing only in which object is updated to record the advance.

The proof proceeds through three lemmas: the physical identification of Û(t) (Lemma VIII.23.A), the two actions of Û(t) corresponding to the two channels (Lemma VIII.23.B), and the preservation of expectation values under either action (Lemma VIII.23.C).

**Lemma VIII.23.A (the evolution operator as the Hilbert-space implementation of x₄'s advance).** The one-parameter unitary group Û(t) = exp(−iĤt/ℏ) is the operator implementation on ℋ of x₄'s advance by ic·t. Specifically: (i) the generator Ĥ is the Noether charge of the temporal uniformity of that advance; (ii) the factor i in the exponent is the perpendicularity marker of x₄, the same i as in x₄ = ict; (iii) the factor ℏ is the action per complete x₄-oscillation cycle at the Planck frequency, as identified in [MG-Constants, §V].

*Proof.* The one-parameter unitary group representing time translations on ℋ is strongly continuous, since physical predictions depend continuously on the elapsed parameter. Stone's theorem therefore gives Û(t) = exp(−itĤ/ℏ) for a densely defined self-adjoint Ĥ, the factor i being forced by the joint requirements of unitarity, Û(t)Û(t)† = 𝟙, and self-adjointness of the generator, Ĥ = Ĥ†. This is the same appeal to Stone's theorem made in Proposition H.2 for the spatial translation subgroup, applied here to the time-translation subgroup; the derivational chain is identical and the group is different. That the i so produced is the i of x₄ = ict is the content of Proposition H.2 together with Proposition III.1: the group whose generators Stone's theorem produces is the isometry group of the Minkowski metric, and the Minkowski metric is the substitution of x₄ = ict into a Euclidean form. Dimensional consistency is immediate — Ĥ carries dimensions of energy and ℏ of action, so Ĥt/ℏ is dimensionless, the factor c being carried inside Ĥ. The identification of ℏ as the action accumulated per complete cycle of the x₄-oscillation at the Planck frequency f_P = 1/t_P is established in [MG-Constants, §V] and is the same identification used in Proposition L.3. That Ĥ is the Noether charge of time-translation symmetry follows from the temporal uniformity of the advance — dx₄/dt = ic holds at every spacetime point, so translation in the parameter is a symmetry of the Principle — by the Noether construction of Remark III.4.1. Every ingredient of Û(t) is therefore fixed by dx₄/dt = ic. ∎

**Lemma VIII.23.B (the two actions of Û(t)).** The unitary Û(t) of Lemma VIII.23.A admits two actions carrying a description of the system at parameter 0 to a description at parameter t:

(B1) *the Channel B action*, in which Û(t) acts on the state, |ψ⟩ ↦ |ψ_S(t)⟩ = Û(t)|ψ⟩, with observables fixed. Geometrically this implements the advance of x₄ as the forward propagation of the state's wavefront, in the sense of Propositions L.1–L.5;

(B2) *the Channel A action*, in which Û(t) acts on the observable by conjugation, Â ↦ Â_H(t) = Û(t)†ÂÛ(t), with the state fixed. Geometrically this implements the advance of x₄ as the carrying of the observable's algebraic definition into the translated frame.

*Proof.* Both actions follow from unitarity. For any operator Ô and any states |ψ⟩, |φ⟩, inserting 𝟙 = Û(t)Û(t)† preserves the matrix element, and the insertions may be distributed across the factors:

⟨φ|Ô|ψ⟩ = ⟨φ|[Û(t)Û(t)†] Ô [Û(t)Û(t)†]|ψ⟩ = ⟨φ_S(t)| Ô_H(t) |ψ_S(t)⟩,

with |ψ_S(t)⟩ = Û(t)|ψ⟩, |φ_S(t)⟩ = Û(t)|φ⟩ and Ô_H(t) = Û(t)†ÔÛ(t). Concentrating both insertions on the state vectors gives the pure Channel B reading; concentrating both on the operator gives the pure Channel A reading; distributing them between the two gives the intermediate pictures, of which the interaction picture is the case in which the free and interacting parts of Ĥ are assigned to the two objects separately. The physical content of the lemma is that the advance of x₄ by ic·t is one process, and that the choice of which object records it is a choice of representation. The two pure readings are the extreme cases of the dual-channel content, and they are equivalent because both record the same advance on the same system. ∎

**Lemma VIII.23.C (preservation of expectation values).** For every |ψ⟩ ∈ ℋ and every observable Â = Â†,

⟨Â⟩(t) := ⟨ψ_S(t)| Â |ψ_S(t)⟩ = ⟨ψ| Â_H(t) |ψ⟩,

independently of which reading is chosen.

*Proof.* Using Û(t)Û(t)† = Û(t)†Û(t) = 𝟙 with the definitions of Lemma VIII.23.B,

⟨ψ_S(t)| Â |ψ_S(t)⟩ = [Û(t)|ψ⟩]† Â [Û(t)|ψ⟩] = ⟨ψ| Û(t)† Â Û(t) |ψ⟩ = ⟨ψ| Â_H(t) |ψ⟩. ∎

*Proof of Theorem VIII.23.1.* Apply Lemma VIII.23.C to the state |ψ⟩ and observable Â of the statement. ∎

**Corollary VIII.23.D (picture independence of every prediction).** Every quantum-mechanical prediction expressible through expectation values — transition probabilities, scattering amplitudes, correlation functions, time-ordered products, S-matrix entries — takes the same value in the Schrödinger picture, in the Heisenberg picture, and in every intermediate picture.

*Proof.* Every such prediction is a sum, product or integral of matrix elements ⟨φ| Ô₁(t₁) ⋯ Ô_n(t_n) |ψ⟩, reducible to expectation-value form by the polarization identities. Each matrix element is picture-independent by Lemmas VIII.23.B and VIII.23.C, the insertions Û(t_k)Û(t_k)† being distributable across states and operators in any consistent way, and the composite is therefore picture-independent as well. ∎

#### VIII.23.3.3 In what sense the proof is geometric

The algebra of Lemma VIII.23.C is the algebra of the textbook proof of the equivalence, found in Schrödinger's third 1926 paper, in von Neumann 1932, in Dirac 1930, and in every modern treatment. The step ⟨Û(t)ψ|Â|Û(t)ψ⟩ = ⟨ψ|Û(t)†ÂÛ(t)|ψ⟩ is the same manipulation in all of them. What distinguishes the present proof is not the algebra but the physical identification of the objects the algebra manipulates, which is supplied by Lemmas VIII.23.A and VIII.23.B.

The textbook proof establishes that the two pictures agree and leaves three questions standing: why the evolution operator has the exponential form it has, why the exponent carries a factor i, and why it carries a factor ℏ. Each is answered here rather than assumed. The exponential form follows from Stone's theorem applied to a strongly continuous unitary representation of the time-translation group, which is a symmetry of the Principle because the advance is uniform. The i is the perpendicularity of x₄ to the three spatial dimensions, entering the exponent by the same route it enters the metric. The ℏ is the action accumulated per complete x₄-cycle. The equality of the two sides is then not an accident of unitary algebra but the statement that two records of one physical advance agree.

Remark VIII.23.3 (the equivalence and the equivalence theorems). The relation of Theorem VIII.23.1 to the classical equivalence results is exactly the relation set out in §VIII.23.1.3. Schrödinger and von Neumann establish that the two pictures agree. Theorem VIII.23.1 establishes why they agree: because both record one advance of x₄, and the two channels of the Principle's content are the two ways of recording it. The classical result is a theorem within quantum mechanics; the present one is a theorem about the descent of quantum mechanics from a geometric principle.

### VIII.23.4 The Causal and Correlational Level: Locality and Nonlocality

#### VIII.23.4.1 The tension

Quantum mechanics carries two features that have coexisted in apparent tension since 1935. On one side it possesses a local operator algebra: observables are defined pointwise, operators at spacelike separation commute, [Ô(x), Ô(y)] = 0 for (x − y)² > 0, and the theory satisfies microcausality in the sense formalized by Wightman, by Haag and Kastler, and by the subsequent algebraic tradition. On the other side it exhibits correlations between spatially separated entangled systems that no local hidden-variable theory reproduces: the singlet correlation E(a,b) = −cos θ_ab violates the Bell inequalities, and the violation is confirmed by the Aspect experiments of the early 1980s and by the loophole-closing experiments of 2015 at Delft, at NIST and in Vienna.

The two features have been developed within separate accounts — microcausality as an axiom of quantum field theory, nonlocal correlation as a result within entanglement theory — and no prior foundation has supplied both as theorems of one principle along disjoint channels of that principle's content.

#### VIII.23.4.2 The reading

*Channel A forces locality and microcausality.* The algebraic-symmetry content — uniformity of the rate, invariance under the isometries, Lorentz covariance of the advance — is what forces the Minkowski metric by Proposition III.1. The Minkowski metric carries the light-cone causal structure, spacelike-separated events are causally disconnected in it, and quantum operators respecting that structure commute at spacelike separation. Microcausality is therefore a Channel A theorem, reached along the same chain that produced the canonical commutation relation in §VIII.6.7: dx₄/dt = ic → Minkowski metric → invariant causal structure → [Ô(x), Ô(y)] = 0 for (x − y)² > 0.

*Channel B forces the nonlocal correlations.* The geometric-propagation content — spherical expansion at rate c from every spacetime point — is what forces Huygens' principle, the McGucken Sphere and the wavefront structure of propagation, by Proposition L.1. The same Sphere that carries the wave aspect of §VIII.23.2 and the path measure of the Lagrangian route carries the correlations of entanglement. Two particles created at a common spacetime event share one McGucken Sphere. Because a photon has v_x4 = 0 — it does not advance in x₄ along a null worldline (§III.6) — the members of an entangled photon pair remain at one x₄-coordinate for all subsequent parameter values regardless of their spatial separation, and the four-dimensional interval between them remains null throughout. Along the null worldline there is neither proper time nor proper distance between the two events. The singlet correlation E(a,b) = −cos θ_ab then follows from the SO(3) Haar-measure symmetry of the shared Sphere, with no local hidden variable required [MG-Twistor, Proposition X.6]. This is the identification recorded in [MG-Equiv]: what presents as nonlocality in three dimensions is the three-dimensional shadow of four-dimensional x₄-coincidence on the light cone.

The two features are therefore two readings of one principle through the same two channels that produced the Hamiltonian and Lagrangian formulations. Their coexistence, understood since Bell 1964 as a genuine feature of the theory requiring reconciliation, is the fourth appearance of the dual-channel separation.

#### VIII.23.4.3 The six senses in which the McGucken Sphere is one object

The Channel B reading rests on a specific geometric claim: that the expanding Sphere is a single object whose spatially separated points share a common identity traceable to one local origin. The claim is established in [MG-Nonlocality, §4] through six frameworks, each identifying the wavefront as a locality in a distinct sense. They are recorded here because each is a statement about the same geometry that §IV.4.4 uses for the caustic bound and that Proposition VI.3 uses for the causal cone.

(i) *Foliation.* The family of Spheres parameterized by t foliates three-space, each Sphere Σ(t) being a leaf. Points on one leaf share a common membership in one submanifold, separated by the transverse structure from points on other leaves.

(ii) *Level sets of a distance function.* The wavefront is the level set d(x) = ct of the distance from the origin of the expansion. Every point on it is equidistant from that origin in the induced metric.

(iii) *Huygens caustic.* The wavefront is the envelope of the secondary wavelets emitted from every point of the preceding wavefront, and therefore the boundary between the region that has received the disturbance and the region that has not. Every point on it shares the same causal status as a boundary point of the causal future of the origin event.

(iv) *Contact geometry.* In the jet space with coordinates (x, y, z, t) the expanding wavefront traces a cone that is a Legendrian submanifold of the contact structure. Membership is fixed by the contact distribution rather than by position alone.

(v) *Conformal and inversive geometry.* Growing spheres map under inversion to spheres or planes, and the family of wavefronts is a pencil in the inversive geometry of space, invariant under the conformal group.

(vi) *Null hypersurfaces.* The five senses above are topological, metric, causal, contact-geometric and conformal. The deepest is Lorentzian. The expanding Sphere in (x₁, x₂, x₃, x₄) with x₄ advancing at ic meets any spatial slice in a sphere of radius growing at c, which is precisely the intersection of a null hypersurface with that slice. Null hypersurfaces are causally extremal — neither spacelike nor timelike — and are the only surfaces along which signals propagate at c. Every point of the Sphere carries the same null-hypersurface identity: all lie on one light cone, connected to the origin and to one another through the null geodesic structure.

The six analyses are mutually reinforcing rather than alternative: each frames one physical object in the language of a different discipline, and each yields the conclusion that the wavefront's spatially separated points share an identity traceable to one local origin. What appears from within a spatial slice as a collection of causally disconnected points is, in the four-dimensional geometry forced by dx₄/dt = ic, one object — in six senses at once.

#### VIII.23.4.4 The two laws

The Channel B reading admits formalization as two laws [MG-Nonlocality, §2].

**The First McGucken Law of Nonlocality.** *All quantum nonlocality begins in locality.* Two systems can exhibit entanglement only if they have shared a common local origin, or if each has interacted locally with members of a system that itself shared one. Equivalently: only particles whose light spheres intersect, each sphere having originated with its own particle, can be entangled. The creation of entanglement is limited by c, even though the correlations found on measuring an entangled pair are instantaneous and persist under subsequent separation. The First Law constrains the creation of entanglement, not its manifestation.

**The Second McGucken Law of Nonlocality.** *Nonlocality grows in a manner limited by c.* As x₄ expands, the Sphere grows; at parameter t after a local event its radius is r = ct. Particles within it may be entangled with that event and particles outside it may not, because the expansion has not reached them. The boundary of entanglement possibility is exactly the light cone.

The Two Laws connect entanglement directly to the causal structure of spacetime. The expansion of x₄ at c generates simultaneously the light cone (Channel A, through the Minkowski metric), the expanding wavefront of wave optics (Channel B, through Huygens' principle) and the sphere of potential entanglement (Channel B, through the shared Sphere). The Two Laws are the Channel B counterpart of the locality axioms of algebraic quantum field theory, which are Channel A consequences; one principle generates both the local structure and the correlations, along its two disjoint channels.

Remark VIII.23.4 (intersecting Spheres and the transfer of entanglement). The First Law accommodates entanglement swapping. Two pairs are created locally, C with D on a Sphere Σ_CD and E with F on a Sphere Σ_EF; C is carried to one location and interacts locally with A, F is carried to another and interacts locally with B, and a Bell measurement on D and E at an intermediate location is itself a local interaction creating a correlation carried on a third Sphere Σ_DE. The three Spheres intersect pairwise at the two measurement events, and the resulting A–B correlation is carried by that chain. Every link traces back to a local creation event, so the correlation is transferred rather than primitive. Along each Sphere in the chain there is neither proper time nor proper distance between the events it carries: the wavefront is one four-dimensional object however far it extends in a spatial slice.

Remark VIII.23.5 (the falsification criterion). The prediction implicit in the Two Laws is stated in [MG-Nonlocality, §3] as a criterion: to falsify the account one would have to entangle two distant, previously unentangled systems with no chain of local contacts and faster than c. Every known protocol for creating entanglement at a distance — swapping, Bell-measurement mediation, teleportation, cascaded parametric down-conversion, cavity-mediated spin-spin entanglement, and the loophole-free tests with sources and analyzers separated by kilometres — proceeds by a chain of locally originated Spheres, with each intersection a local interaction at which the correlation is transferred. The account predicts that no protocol without such a chain can exist, because the creation of entanglement is a Channel B process requiring a shared Sphere and Channel A confines that Sphere's expansion to the light cone. The boundary of entanglement creation and the boundary of causal contact are then the same surface, which is the content of the criterion.

### VIII.23.5 The Four Levels Together

The separation of §III.8 has now been applied at four levels of description, each addressing a different question about quantum mechanics.

| Level | Question addressed | Channel A reading | Channel B reading | Developed in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Formulation | Which formulation of quantum mechanics is primary? | Hamiltonian: operator algebra, Stone's theorem, canonical commutation relation (Propositions H.1–H.5) | Lagrangian: Huygens wavefronts, path measure, Feynman kernel (Propositions L.1–L.6) | §VIII.6.7 |
| 2. Dynamics | How does a quantum system evolve? | Heisenberg picture: observables carried into the translated frame, Â_H(t) = Û(t)†ÂÛ(t) | Schrödinger picture: the state's wavefront carried forward, \|ψ_S(t)⟩ = Û(t)\|ψ⟩ | §VIII.23.3, Theorem VIII.23.1 |
| 3. Ontology | What is a quantum object? | Particle aspect: eigenvalue events, quantized exchange, localized detection | Wave aspect: interference, diffraction, λ_dB = h/p | §VIII.23.2 |
| 4. Causality | How do quantum objects stand to the causal structure of spacetime? | Locality: microcausality, [Ô(x), Ô(y)] = 0 at spacelike separation | Nonlocality: the shared McGucken Sphere, E(a,b) = −cos θ_ab, the Two Laws | §VIII.23.4 |

The four rows are not four independent observations. Each is the same separation of the Principle's content applied to a different question, and the agreement across the four is what makes the separation itself credible: a distinction drawn to explain one coexistence, which then explains three further coexistences without modification, is doing work rather than being fitted.

The ordering of the rows is one of increasing depth. Level 1 concerns the mathematical machinery used to compute. Level 2 concerns time evolution within a given machinery. Level 3 concerns the character of the object itself. Level 4 concerns how objects stand to the causal structure of spacetime, and is the deepest of the four because the coexistence of locality with nonlocal correlation is precisely the feature Einstein identified in 1935 and Bell analysed in 1964 as the most distinctive of the theory. That one separation of content generates all four coexistences — including the most contested — is what the four-level table is offered as evidence for.

Two consequences follow for the program of the present paper.

The first concerns the reach of Theorem VI.1. The four-fold uniqueness theorem consumes both channels, as the audit in §III.8.2 records: Channel A supplies the Poincaré invariance of Proposition IV.1, the Clifford structure and phase indeterminacy of Propositions V.1 and VI.2; Channel B supplies the causal cone that Schuller closure takes as input in Proposition VI.3 and the Compton coupling of the matter orientation condition. A framework carrying one channel could not reach four sectors, for the same reason it could not reach four coexistences.

The second concerns the standing of the whole. By the criterion of §VIII.23.1.5, a framework is a foundation rather than a reformulation if its principle is not a rewriting of what it derives, if the known results follow as consequences, and if at least one important result is overdetermined. The four instances catalogued there — the commutation relation, Huygens' principle, the Second Law and the Einstein–Hilbert sector — satisfy the third condition four times over. The four levels of the present section show that the property responsible for the overdetermination, namely the presence of two channels in one geometric-dynamical statement, is not a fortunate accident of one derivation but the organizing feature of the framework, visible wherever quantum mechanics presents a pair of coexisting descriptions that the received account leaves unexplained.

## VIII.24 Two Conceptions of the Foundations of Physics: A Meta-Theory of All Possible Models, and the One Physical Reality From Which the Laws Deduce

Everything below descends from the physical expansion of x₄. The McGucken Principle states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the rate c from every point of spacetime, dx₄/dt = ic. Section VIII.22 set the results of the Assumptions of Physics programme beside the theorems of that Principle terminus by terminus. The present section addresses something prior to any particular terminus: what the two programmes take the word *foundations* to mean. The difference is stated plainly by the programme's own author, it is deliberate, and it accounts for the pattern of §VIII.22 better than any comparison of individual results does.

Quotations in this section are from the programme's 2025–26 overview lecture [AoP-Overview], whose auto-generated transcript has been lightly repunctuated without alteration of wording; the timestamps are given so that each may be checked against the recording.

### VIII.24.1 The programme's aim, in its own words

The constructive aim is stated at the opening: "the main goal of assumptions of physics is to identify a handful of physical starting points from which the basic laws can be rigorously derived" (0:11), with the further requirement that the mathematics itself — "real numbers, topology, sigma algebra and all that" (2:08) — be recovered from physical requirements rather than assumed. That aim is Einsteinian in form, and it is the aim §VIII.22 engages.

The meta-aim is different, and it is stated with equal directness. The received view in the field, as the lecture characterizes it, is "that all the theory that we have right now are approximation of the one true theory," and that "the goal of physics is to find this one true law of the universe" (2:52–2:58). The programme sets that view aside: "saying that the goal of physics is to find the true laws of the universe is not a very productive point" (5:02). What replaces it is stated at 5:36–5:55:

> "the foundations of physics basically mean to find the theory of physical models. Not to find the one true model that rules them all, but is to find a meta theory for all the possible physical models."

The model for that ambition is drawn explicitly from another discipline: "in the foundations of mathematics nobody's looking for the theorem of everything, the theorem that proves all the theorem. No, doing foundations in mathematics means doing logic, doing model theory" (7:03–7:18), with the same point made for the foundations of computer science, where the object of study is what computation is rather than an algorithm that subsumes all algorithms.

The same posture governs the treatment of limits. On the recovery of classical mechanics at high entropy and of non-relativistic mechanics at low speed, the lecture states: "in both cases you see that there is no mechanism for this limit" (37:44), and "there's no mechanism required for being at high entropy" (38:27). The absence of a mechanism is presented as a feature, since it frees the classical limit from any dependence on decoherence or on any other particular process.

None of this is a shortcoming of execution. It is a considered choice of what the subject is, and within that choice the programme's results — the equivalence of the action principle to (DR), (IND) and (KE); the classical uncertainty bound σ_q σ_p ≥ ℏ/e; the recovery of topology and the σ-algebras from experimental verifiability; the identification, at 41:21, that "all the geometric structure that we have in both classical mechanics and quantum mechanics are essentially there exactly to tell you what the entropy of statistical ensembles is" — are rigorous and are, in several cases, results no one else has obtained.

### VIII.24.2 The other conception, and what deduces from one reality

The conception this paper works within is the older one, and it is the one the programme names as unproductive for its purposes. Einstein put it in 1918, in the address for Planck's sixtieth birthday: "The supreme task of the physicist is to arrive at those universal elementary laws from which the cosmos can be built up by pure deduction" [Einstein 1918]. Wheeler put the same expectation as a prediction about what the answer would feel like when found — "behind it all is surely an idea so simple, so beautiful, that when we grasp it — in a decade, a century, or a millennium — we will all say to each other, how could it have been otherwise?" — quoted as the epigraph of §I of this paper. Neither statement is a claim about a catalog of possible models. Both are claims that there is one physical reality and that the laws are what follow from it.

The McGucken Principle is a proposal of exactly that kind. It names a physical ontology in one sentence — the fourth dimension is expanding at the rate c from every point of spacetime — and everything the present paper contains is deduction from it. The inventory below is not new material; it is a map of what has already been derived and where, assembled so that the shape of the claim is visible in one place.

| What is deduced | From what content of the Principle | Where in this paper |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The Minkowski metric and its signature | x₄ = ict substituted into the Euclidean form; Channel A | Proposition III.1 |
| The invariance of c and the relativity principle — Einstein's two 1905 postulates, as theorems | The rate is the same in every frame; Lorentz covariance of that rate | §III.3; §VIII.18 |
| The four-speed budget u^μu_μ = −c² and the four-fold ontology | The advance is spent between the three dimensions and the fourth | Proposition III.2; §III.6 |
| The ten Poincaré conservation laws, and local gauge invariance | Uniformity and isometry invariance of the advance; x₄-phase indeterminacy | Remark III.4.1; Proposition III.5 |
| The free-particle sector | Poincaré invariance, reparametrization invariance, locality; the x₄-advance field X with ∂_μX∂^μX = −1 | Proposition IV.1; §IV.4 |
| The Dirac matter sector | Clifford structure forced by the signature, with Compton coupling to the advance | Proposition V.1 |
| The Yang-Mills gauge sector | Local x₄-phase invariance | Proposition VI.2 |
| The Einstein-Hilbert sector, by two disjoint routes | The universal causal cone as Schuller-closure input; the horizon mode count on null hypersurfaces | Proposition VI.3; Remark VI.3.1 |
| The Higgs sector, as a derived fifth sector | The x₄-advance field's potential in the broken phase | Theorem VI.2 |
| The full uniqueness theorem over all five sectors | Both channels jointly | Theorems VI.1 and VI.2 |
| Quantum mechanics — the commutation relation, the Schrödinger equation, the path integral, the Born rule, the uncertainty relation | Channel A and Channel B, by two disjoint chains | §VIII.6.7; §VIII.8; §VIII.11 |
| The pictures, aspects and correlations of quantum mechanics — Heisenberg and Schrödinger, particle and wave, local algebra and Bell correlation | One separation of the Principle's contents, read at three further levels | §VIII.23.2–§VIII.23.4 |
| The constants c and ℏ | Rate of the advance; action per oscillation cycle | §VIII.10 |
| Thermodynamics — entropy's increase, the entropy floor, the scale that sets the zero | The advance is spherically symmetric, directional, and quantized per Compton cycle | §VIII.14; §VIII.22.5; [MG-Thermo] |
| Time, and all five of its arrows | The advance is +ic and never −ic | §VIII.14; §VIII.16.5 |

Two features of the inventory bear on the comparison of §VIII.24.1.

The first is that every row is a deduction from one sentence rather than an assumption selected to fit a case. The programme's method assembles, for each law, the minimal set of assumptions equivalent to it, and its own results then show those assumption sets to be recombinations of a small number of conditions. The method of this paper starts one level below the assumptions and asks what physical fact they are assumptions *about*. Where the programme's ledger has an assumption, this paper's ledger has a consequence, and the consequence is the same in every case where both have an entry.

The second is that the mechanism the programme declines to seek is what the rows supply. The classical limit is a case in point. The programme establishes that classical mechanics is the high-entropy limit of quantum mechanics and states, correctly within its method, that no mechanism is required for a system to be at high entropy. The Principle does not contradict that; it adds what the method does not ask for, namely why there is an entropy floor at all — the advance of x₄ is quantized at one Compton cycle, so the state count cannot fall below one, and ℏ → 0 is the limit in which the cycle falls below resolution (§VIII.22.5). A framework that seeks no mechanism will not find one; a framework that seeks one may.

### VIII.24.3 Three further termini named in the overview

The overview states three open problems that §VIII.22.7's ledger did not previously carry. Each is quoted, then answered.

**The count of states in the continuum.** Three requirements — that a single state have measure one, that a finite continuous range carry finite measure, and that the count be additive over disjoint sets — are shown to be jointly unsatisfiable: "all these three things are incompatible in the continuum," so "we have to pick two" (1:16:31–1:16:49). Quantum mechanics is then read as resolving the conflict by defining the count as the exponential of the entropy, giving measure one to a pure state and additivity only on orthogonal states (1:17:13–1:19:16). The conjecture drawn from this is that quantum gravity must impose the corresponding bound on the count of degrees of freedom per unit volume — "it has to put a lower bound on the count of degrees of freedom" (1:19:54) — with the closing worry that "maybe we don't even have the correct math to actually create a theory of quantum gravity" (1:22:13).

Under the Principle the count is not a measure chosen among three incompatible requirements but a count of x₄-advance states, and its properties follow from the advance rather than from a selection. The advance proceeds at one Planck wavelength λ_P per Planck time (§VIII.10), so a finite spatial volume carries finitely many advance states, which is the second requirement; a state occupying one complete cycle counts as one, which is the first; and additivity holds exactly between regions whose advance states are separately resolvable, failing where they are not, which is the third requirement in the restricted form quantum mechanics is observed to satisfy. The lower bound on the count per unit volume that the conjecture seeks for quantum gravity is the horizon area law in Planck units, already derived from the Principle for black-hole horizons in [MG-Bekenstein] and for cosmological horizons in [MG-FRW-Holography], with the de Sitter identity ρ_P/ρ_Λ = (8/3)S of §VIII.21.8 as its cosmological instance. The requirement the conjecture names is therefore a consequence of a Planck-scale advance rather than an additional axiom to be found, and it is available at the same time as the quantum-mechanical bound because both are counts of the same thing. The measure that carries the count in field theory is written explicitly at §VIII.25.4, where the compactness of the x₄-fibre Brillouin zone is what bounds the ultraviolet while leaving the Lorentz symmetry exact — the closing worry that the required mathematics may not exist is answered by a measure, not by new mathematics.

**The unitary group of quantum mechanics is too large.** The lecture observes that "every continuous linear operator defined on the whole Hilbert space is bounded," so that "position, momentum, energy, number of particles, all these" cannot be defined on the whole Hilbert space (51:50), and draws the conclusion at 52:17–52:30: the unitary group "is too big. It's moving things from physical to unphysical. We probably don't have the right group which probably means we don't have the right symmetry."

The diagnosis is that the group has been postulated rather than generated, and the Principle answers it in that form. The group of physical evolutions is the group Stone's theorem produces from the isometries of the metric that x₄ = ict forces, as established in Proposition H.2 of §VIII.6.7 and used in Lemma VIII.23.A; the states are those the advance actually reaches. The one-parameter unitary group of Lemma VIII.23.A is not selected from among all unitaries on an abstract Hilbert space but is the Hilbert-space implementation of the advance by ic·t, with the generator fixed as the Noether charge of the advance's uniformity and both constants in its exponent fixed by §VIII.10. The cogeneration of Minkowski space and Hilbert space from the single Principle [MG-MinkHilb] is the general form of this answer: on that construction the two spaces arise together rather than as independently posited structures between which a mismatch of size can arise, and the symmetry the lecture suspects is missing is the one the advance carries. In the field-theoretic setting the same answer is the derivation of the Wightman axioms W0 and W1 together, recorded at §VIII.25.5.

**Vectors, units, and infinitesimal displacement.** The lecture objects to the standard definition of tangent vectors as derivations on the ground that "in physics all the real numbers have to have units" (47:17), so that components taken along a radial and an angular coordinate carry different units and cannot be summed, with the difficulty unresolvable in phase space; it notes that "the old way of defining vectors in terms of infinitesimal displacement actually works a lot better though it is being disused" (48:19).

The Principle is stated in exactly the disused form. dx₄/dt = ic is an infinitesimal displacement per unit of the parameter, carrying units of length per unit time, and the coordinate it advances, x₄ = ict, carries units of length like the three it is perpendicular to. The all-length convention adopted in §IV.4.1 and used throughout the free sector is not a notational preference but the form the Principle takes; the constraint ∂_μX∂^μX = −1 on the advance field is dimensionless because X = cτ is a length. What the objection identifies as the better definition is the one the geometry supplies.

### VIII.24.4 What the difference amounts to

The two programmes are not competitors within one conception of the subject. They are two answers to a prior question about what the subject is. The Assumptions of Physics programme takes the conception drawn from the foundations of mathematics: a meta-theory of all possible physical models, cataloguing which physical assumptions are equivalent to which laws, deliberately declining a single true model and deliberately declining the search for mechanism. This paper takes the conception Einstein stated as the supreme task and Wheeler expected to be simple when found: one physical reality, named, from which the laws of this universe follow by deduction. Each conception yields what it is built to yield. The programme's results are the reason its termini are legible at all — without its map of where the received formalism runs out, a physical source at each point would have nowhere to land (§VIII.22.8).

The difference does have one consequence worth stating without hedging, and it is a consequence of method rather than a criticism of anyone: one of the disadvantages of not looking for a physical model from which mathematical physics derives is that one often does not find that which one is not looking for.

## VIII.25 The McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic Reading of the Lagrangian in Quantum Field Theory: What the Advance Supplies Above the Classical Density

Everything below descends from the physical expansion of x₄. The McGucken Principle states that the fourth dimension is expanding at the rate c from every point of spacetime, dx₄/dt = ic. Sections IV through VI force the five sectors of ℒ_McG and prove their uniqueness, and they stop at the classical Lagrangian density. That is one rung below where a Lagrangian is actually used. In practice the density is quantized, and the standard route to the quantum theory imposes a second layer of postulates on top of the classical one: promote the field to an operator, impose canonical commutation or anticommutation relations by hand, adopt the Fock space, and accept the axioms of the resulting field theory as axioms. A Lagrangian forced by a physical principle, followed by a quantization procedure not forced by anything, is a partial result.

The present section closes that rung. Its material is developed at length in [MG-QFT]; what follows states the part of it the Lagrangian program uses, in five results and a scope statement. Each subsection opens with what the advance predicts about the item under discussion.

### VIII.25.1 What quantization ordinarily costs, and what the advance supplies instead

The advance is a physical fact about spacetime rather than about a formalism, so it does not stop being in force when a classical field is replaced by an operator-valued one. The x₄-advance continues at ic at every event whether the field at that event is written as a function or as an operator, which is why the passage to the quantum theory is not a place where new postulates about the world are needed.

The standard construction of the second-quantized Dirac field proceeds in five steps: promote ψ(x) to an operator-valued field Ψ̂(x); expand in plane waves with coefficient operators a, a†, b, b†; impose {a_p, a†_q} = (2π)³δ³(p − q) by hand; take the resulting Fock space as the physical many-particle Hilbert space; accept the Pauli exclusion principle as a consequence of antisymmetry. The spin-statistics theorem of Pauli 1940 and of Lüders–Zumino and Burgoyne 1958 supplies a retrospective justification — given Lorentz invariance, microcausality and a positive energy spectrum, half-integer-spin fields must be quantized with anticommutators — and the theorem is rigorous. It establishes what nature must do given the other axioms, and it does not derive the anticommutation from a physical fact.

Under the Principle the ordering inverts, and the inversion is the subject of §VIII.25.2.

### VIII.25.2 Second quantization as a theorem chain

The advance predicts that the exchange of two identical matter fields is not a free convention: matter is oriented in x₄ by the matter orientation condition (M) of Postulate III.4.P, and a spinor carried once around the McGucken Sphere returns with the opposite sign, so the exchange of two such fields carries a definite sign fixed by the geometry rather than by a choice of quantization scheme.

The chain established in [MG-QFT, §14.9], drawing on [MG-Dirac], runs as follows.

The McGucken-Sphere SO(3) lifted onto Cl(1,3)⁺ Weyl spinors acts on the spinor representation by the half-angle factor exp(iθΣ/2), so a rotation by θ = 2π acts as exp(iπΣ) = −𝟙 and only a rotation by 4π acts as the identity. This is the 4π-periodicity of spinor rotation, and in this framework it is a consequence of the Sphere geometry lifted to Cl(1,3)⁺ rather than an imported group-theoretic fact about the SU(2) → SO(3) covering.

For N indistinguishable spin-½ matter fields on a spatial slice, the exchange of two of them is a closed path in the configuration space C_N = {(**x**₁, …, **x**_N) ∈ (ℝ³)^N : **x**_i ≠ **x**_j}, realized on the substrate by transporting one field around the other along a loop on the McGucken Sphere of the centre-of-mass event. The 4π-periodicity applied to that path gives holonomy −𝟙, so ψ(p₁, p₂) ↦ −ψ(p₂, p₁), and the physical subspace is the antisymmetric one, 𝓕_phys = ∧^N(ℋ₁).

The canonical anticommutation relations then follow by applying that antisymmetry to the operator actions on physical states, rather than being imposed. The Pauli exclusion principle follows from (a†_p)² = ½{a†_p, a†_p} = 0. The spin-statistics rule follows from the same source: the geometry selects the fermionic spin structure on the identical-particle configuration space, which forces antisymmetric exchange, which forces anticommutation.

Two consequences for the present paper. First, ℒ_matter of Proposition V.1 carries its own quantization: the Lagrangian and the statistics of the field it describes descend from the same condition (M), so the classical density and the quantization rule are not two independent inputs. Second, the Feynman propagator's iε prescription acquires the reading already given at §VIII.7 for the Wick rotation — positive-frequency modes are matter-oriented and propagate forward in x₄, negative-frequency modes are antimatter-oriented and propagate backward, and the +iε selects dx₄/dt = +ic rather than −ic. Time-ordering in the propagator is the directionality of the advance.

### VIII.25.3 Gauge redundancy as per-event structure, and the second answer to §VIII.22.10

The advance holds at every event and is not referenced to any global orientation: there is a +ic direction at each event, related between events by the Lorentz transformation law, and no absolute orientation anywhere. A description of the fields must therefore carry a freedom corresponding to the choice of reference at each event, and that freedom is gauge redundancy.

Section VIII.22.10 reached this conclusion from one direction, answering a question about the potential θ of the state-flow. The same conclusion is reached in [MG-QFT, §14.16.1] from a different direction, answering a question about why gauge redundancy exists at all. David Tong, in a June 2025 interview:

> "You are obliged when you write down the laws of physics to introduce things that somehow are unphysical. That's the gauge principle. We elevate it to this fancy name. We call it gauge symmetry. It's a terrible name. It's gauge redundancy. It's a redundancy in our description of the laws of physics. I don't really understand why the laws of physics are like this." [MG-QFT, §14.16.1, quoting Tong]

The answer given there is that each event carries its own x₄ structure in its fibre, that the U(1) fibre over an event is the x₄-phase circle, and that the gauge potential A_μ encodes a choice of section across the bundle of per-event phase circles with no section privileged, because no absolute x₄-orientation is part of the physics. A gauge transformation A → A + dλ redefines the per-event phase reference. Tong's own illustration — that the overall voltage does not matter and only differences do, with A₀ identified as voltage — is the same fact at the level of the received formalism: what is physical is the difference between per-event references across the manifold, which is the field strength.

The two answers are independent in the sense of §VIII.23.1.1. The chain of §VIII.22.10 starts from determinism and reversibility on an extended phase space, passes through the divergence-free displacement field, the potential θ, and the identity θ|_L = −mc dX, and arrives at the phase freedom. The chain of [MG-QFT, §14.16.1] starts from the per-event structure of the advance and its bundle, and arrives at the same freedom without passing through (DR), through θ, or through Hamilton–Jacobi theory at all. One conjecture about Lagrangian mechanics and one question about why gauge theories exist receive the same answer along disjoint routes, which by the criterion of §VIII.23.1.5 is the pattern that distinguishes a consequence from an artifact of a derivational choice.

The same source reads two further coexistences that the received account leaves unexplained. Tong's statement that in quantum field theory one can make locality manifest or unitarity manifest but not both is, in the vocabulary of §III.8, the two channels: locality is the visibility of Channel B, since propagation bounded by c is what the McGucken Sphere supplies, and unitarity is the visibility of Channel A, since Stone's theorem on the derived Hilbert space is what conservation of the norm expresses. This is the same separation applied at a fifth level, after the four of §VIII.23.5.

### VIII.25.4 The state count in field theory: the hybrid measure

The advance proceeds at one Planck wavelength per Planck tick, so the count of states in a finite region is finite and is fixed by the advance rather than chosen among incompatible conventions.

Section VIII.22.6 recorded the programme's open call for a count of states that survives the passage to field theory, and §VIII.24.3 answered the count-of-states trilemma in general terms. The concrete construction is the hybrid measure of [MG-QFT, §§3.6–3.8]:

ℳ = ℝ³ × (ℓ_P · ℤ),

continuous in the three spatial directions and discrete in the x₄ fibre over each event, with the conjugate momentum in the fibre direction confined to a compact Brillouin zone 𝔹 = [−πℏ/ℓ_P, +πℏ/ℓ_P]. Loop integrals are bounded in the k₄ direction by the compactness of 𝔹, and the dispersion relation carries the continuum form in the spatial momenta with a sin² correction only in k₄:

E² = c²|**p**|² + (2ℏc/ℓ_P)² sin²(k₄ℓ_P/2ℏ) + m²c⁴.

Because the discreteness is in the fibre over each event rather than in a lattice laid across space, a Lorentz boost acts on the per-event +ic directions by the ordinary transformation law on four-vectors and the fibre structure transforms with it; no preferred frame appears and no Lorentz-violating operators are generated. The measure therefore does what a regulator is ordinarily unable to do, which is to bound the theory in the ultraviolet while leaving the symmetry exact.

This is the field-theoretic form of the count the trilemma of §VIII.24.3 requires. A finite spatial volume carries finitely many advance states, since the fibre over each event is a copy of ℓ_P · ℤ; a single complete cycle counts as one; and additivity holds between regions whose advance states are separately resolvable. The scale ℓ_P is fixed as in §VIII.10, and the scope condition attaching to it is stated at §VIII.25.6.

### VIII.25.5 The Wightman axioms as consequences

The advance fixes a direction, +ic and never −ic, at every event, and the algebraic residue of that directionality is the condition that the energy-momentum spectrum lies in the closed forward cone.

Standard axiomatic field theory takes six statements as axioms: a separable complex Hilbert space (W0); a strongly continuous unitary representation of the Poincaré group on it (W1); commutation or anticommutation of field operators at spacelike separation (W2); field operators as operator-valued distributions on a dense domain (W3); a unique Poincaré-invariant vacuum (W4); and the spectrum condition Spec(P^μ) ⊂ V̄₊ (W5). [MG-QFT, §15.11] derives all six from the Principle, with W2 and W5 proved there and W0, W1, W3, W4 proved in [MG-MinkHilb], the spectrum condition appearing as the algebraic residue of the +ic orientation. Section §VIII.24.3 recorded a request for the group of a field theory to be generated rather than postulated; W0 and W1 derived together are that request answered in the field-theoretic setting, since the Hilbert space and the group acting on it come from one source instead of being posited separately and then matched.

### VIII.25.6 Scope

Five limits, stated so that the section is not read wider than it is.

*The Newtonian constant.* The hybrid measure of §VIII.25.4 rests on the Principle's oscillatory clause — one quantum of action per cycle of the expansion, Clause III.3.P — together with the Schwarzschild self-consistency condition that fixes the wavelength at ℓ_P through Newton's G. The action per cycle is part of what the Principle states; what the measure additionally requires is G, which is the one empirical constant the framework retains (Remark III.3.1). [MG-QFT, (L10)] records as open the sharper question of whether the self-consistency step can be run without G as an independent input. This does not affect §§IV–VI, which do not use the measure.

*The Wightman derivations are graded at the single-particle level.* [MG-QFT, §15.11] states the six axioms as derived at the single-particle level, with the multi-particle Fock-space extension to the full continuum content open.

*What the gauge-group derivation leaves empirical.* [MG-QFT, §9] derives G_SM = U(1)_Y × SU(2)_L × SU(3)_c together with sin²θ_W = 3/8 at substrate scale, and adds a No-GUT result excluding the embedding of 𝔤_SM in 𝔰𝔲(5), 𝔰𝔬(10), 𝔢₆ or 𝔢₈. It does not supply the three-generation structure, the PMNS and CKM mixing matrices or the CP-violating phase ([MG-QFT, (L4)]), nor the numerical values of v, λ, m_h and the individual Yukawa couplings ([MG-QFT, (L3)]), nor the radiative stability of μ² ([MG-QFT, (L5)]). The existence of a non-vanishing vacuum expectation value is settled topologically; its magnitude is open.

*Confinement is relocated, not resolved.* The derivation of the colour gauge group does not supply the string tension, the hadron spectrum or the chiral-symmetry-breaking pattern, and the Yang-Mills mass gap retains its difficulty ([MG-QFT, §§15.1–15.3, (L1)]).

*Per-sector microcausality.* The microcausality theorem supplies the geometry; verification across every gauge, fermion, Higgs and mixed sector is sketched rather than exhaustively formalized ([MG-QFT, (L8)]).

### VIII.25.7 What the rung adds to the argument of this paper

Sections IV through VI establish that the five sectors of ℒ_McG are forced. Taken alone, that result is compatible with a reader's objection that the quantum theory built on the density reintroduces by postulate what the density derives — operator promotion, the canonical relations, the Fock space, the axioms. The material above removes the objection at four points: the statistics and the canonical anticommutation relations descend from the same condition (M) that fixes ℒ_matter; the gauge redundancy of every sector descends from the per-event structure of the advance, along a route disjoint from the one §VIII.22.10 uses; the ultraviolet behaviour is bounded by a measure that leaves the symmetry exact; and the axioms of the resulting field theory are consequences rather than starting points. The Lagrangian and the quantum field theory built on it descend from one statement, and the scope conditions of §VIII.25.6 mark where that descent is not yet complete.

## IX. Conclusion

The Lagrangian of physics — the single scalar functional from which all equations of motion follow by variational extremization — has been sought since Maupertuis’s 1744 formulation of the Principle of Least Action. Each stage of the historical development (Euler’s variational calculus, Lagrange’s analytical mechanics, Hamilton’s canonical formulation, Noether’s symmetry-conservation correspondence, Hilbert’s gravitational action, Dirac’s relativistic matter Lagrangian, Yang and Mills’s non-Abelian gauge principle, Feynman’s path-integral reformulation, Witten’s M-theory proposal) has supplied the Lagrangian of the then-accepted theory. At each stage, the specific form of the Lagrangian has been the empirical and theory of the theory. At each stage, the question of what principle forces the Lagrangian has remained open.

The McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic supplies the missing principle. Theorem VI.1 establishes that the full Lagrangian of physics — the free-particle kinetic term, the Dirac matter sector, the Yang-Mills gauge sector, and the Einstein-Hilbert gravitational sector — is uniquely determined by the McGucken Principle combined with standard field-theoretic consistency requirements. Each sector is forced by its own uniqueness subtheorem (Propositions IV.1, V.1, VI.2, VI.3), and the consistency requirements used in the subtheorems are either consequences of the McGucken Principle or standard requirements of any field theory. The composite Lagrangian ℒ_McG is therefore determined by one geometric postulate, not by the long list of independent postulates that underlie the Standard Model Lagrangian and the Einstein-Hilbert action separately.

What distinguishes the McGucken Lagrangian from its predecessors — the Einstein-Hilbert Lagrangian of 1915, the Dirac Lagrangian of 1928, the Yang-Mills Lagrangian of 1954, the Standard Model Lagrangian of 1967–1973, the hypothetical M-theory Lagrangian of 1995-present — is that its form is derived. Maupertuis in 1744 asked what nature minimizes; Euler, Lagrange, Hamilton, Hilbert, Dirac, Yang, Mills, Weinberg, Salam, and Witten each supplied a Lagrangian for the physics of their era. The McGucken Principle answers Maupertuis’s question in a specific form: nature minimizes the negative accumulated advance of the fourth dimension along the worldline, −mc∫|dx₄|, plus the gauge-invariant couplings of matter and fields to x₄’s advance, plus the curvature modulation of that advance on a curved background. All of physics — classical mechanics, relativity, quantum mechanics, gauge theory, and gravitation — is the variational extremization of this single scalar, with the form of the scalar forced by dx₄/dt = ic.

Three observations frame the conclusion.

First, the McGucken Lagrangian is the Lovelock-uniqueness generalization to all of fundamental physics. Lovelock 1971 established that the gravitational Lagrangian is forced once diffeomorphism invariance is adopted. The McGucken dx₄/dt = ic framework establishes that all four sectors of the Lagrangian are forced once the McGucken Principle is adopted, because each sector’s uniqueness subtheorem uses only invariance principles and standard field-theoretic requirements that are themselves consequences of the Principle. The advance is not a new Lagrangian but a proof that the known Lagrangian of physics is uniquely determined by one geometric postulate.

Second, the M-theory Lagrangian problem is resolved. For thirty years, M-theory has been defined only through its limits (five perturbative superstring theories plus 11D supergravity), without a fundamental Lagrangian formulation. The McGucken Lagrangian ℒ_McG is the M-theory Lagrangian: the physical principle underlying M-theory is dx₄/dt = ic, and its Lagrangian formulation is the four-sector ℒ_McG derived in this paper. Witten’s 1995 naming of M-theory preceded the identification of what M-theory is; the identification has now been made, and its Lagrangian has now been written.

Third, the 282-year tradition of Lagrangian methods — from Maupertuis’s 1744 metaphysical Principle of Least Action through the modern Standard Model — is completed by the identification of the underlying geometric principle that forces the Lagrangian. Maupertuis conjectured that nature operates with maximum economy; the McGucken Principle specifies what nature is economizing: the fourth dimension is advancing at the velocity of light, and every physical process is the variational extremization of that advance subject to the appropriate matter, gauge, and gravitational couplings. The economy Maupertuis intuited is now identified with the geometric economy of x₄’s advance. The Lagrangian that Euler, Lagrange, Hamilton, Hilbert, Dirac, Yang, Mills, and Witten each supplied piece by piece is now assembled in a single form, forced by one principle.

Maupertuis’s 1750 Essai de cosmologie contained the epigraph that opens this paper: “If anyone were to give an account of a science, he must first describe the action.” The present paper has described the action. It is ℒ_McG. It is forced by dx₄/dt = ic. It is the unique McGucken Lagrangian.

The 2025 Summer School lectures, https://assumptionsofphysics.org/presentations/2025SummerSchool.html — “Reverse Physics for classical mechanics” for the statements of (DR), (IND), (KE) and infinitesimal reducibility; “Open problems in Reverse Physics” for the field-theory generalization sought (“from finitely many DOFs to continuously many DOFs,” a “suitable Hamiltonian/symplectic formulation of field theory (starting with electromagnetism),” and how to “generalize count of states/entropy … to field theory”); and “Open problems in Physical Mathematics” for the generalized-Poisson questions (“Can we define non-commutativity of variables based on non-commutativity of composed transformations?”, “What new axiom is needed (if at all)?”). “Reverse Physics for GR,” https://assumptionsofphysics.org/presentations/20241116-UMichRelativity.pdf, for the conjecture that general relativity is equivalent to determinism/reversibility plus degree-of-freedom independence for infinitely many dense degrees of freedom, and for the metric entering as a given structure. G. Carcassi, F. Calderón and C. A. Aidala, “Experimental verifiability and topology,” arXiv:2103.06053, for the derivation of T₀ second-countable topologies and σ-algebras from experimental verifiability, and the Fisher–Rao metric on ensemble space from the strict concavity of entropy, cited in §VIII.22.3. The four-step third-law argument of §VIII.22.5 is from the CORE1 lecture, G. Carcassi, “It’s all about the entropy,” Assumptions of Physics (2026).

McGucken, Thermodynamics Derived from the McGucken Principle: Chain of Theorems of dx₄/dt = ic — Resolving Einstein’s Unease, the Probability Measure as Haar Measure, Ergodicity as Huygens-Wavefront Identity. elliotmcguckenphysics.com (April 24, 2026). Cited in §VIII.22.5 for Theorem 22.5 and Theorem 22.5.3: the third-law classical uncertainty bound with h grounded as the action per Compton cycle, the physical reading of S ≥ 0 as the one-cycle resolution limit, and the cycle-occupation origin of the 2/e ratio.

[MG-Noether] E. McGucken, “The McGucken Principle of a Fourth Expanding Dimension Exalts and Unifies The Conservation Laws: How the Symmetries of Noether’s Theorem, the Conservation Laws of the Poincaré, U(1), SU(2), SU(3), Diffeomorphism Groups, and the Imaginary Structure of Quantum Theory and Complexification of Physics arise from dx₄/dt = ic,” elliotmcguckenphysics.com (April 21, 2026).
